no model in the validator slot) OR an independent judge Responder (judge-family disjoint from the drafter, finding #42 — not a model grading its own work). `best_of_n/` untouched; loop, correction, records (Draft/Candidate/Verdict/Solved/Exhausted) are its own — composition, no new vocabulary. **Dual contract:** artifact (ruff + mypy clean) + signal (reuses best_of_n's kinds). **Observation contract, both legs:** CI — `tests/test_best_of_n_verified.py`, three deterministic paths (Solved: N pass → select; Exhausted: all fail → correction round 2 (rounds {1,2}) → give up; independent-judge-model branch: a Responder verify with a PASS-menu → parsed pass → Solved) + the runtime's own `result.status` asserted + no invented kinds; walkthrough — `run_best_of_n_verified.py --model kimi-k2.6:cloud --verifier-model glm-5.1:cloud` drafted 3 vowel-counter one-liners with kimi, verified each with glm (independent cross-family judge), selected r1/2 → replayable record (`/tmp/bon-walkthrough`). **Full suite: 582 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed** with mypy on PATH (the 77-apparent-failures gotcha is a subprocess-PATH artifact, not code — now in the WORKING_AGREEMENT close command + the workspace gotcha memory). **Rubber Duck Pass:** *Sequence* — seeder fans 3 Drafts; drafter emits Candidate+ModelUsage per Draft; validator verifies each → Verdict; the judge fires ONCE when all N verdicts of the round land, selects the first passing → Solved; threshold_count(Solved,1) finalises. *Observations (six):* missing pair — none; order — the judge correctly waits for all N verdicts (predicate `>= n`) before selecting; vocabulary gap — none (best_of_n's records); payload anomaly — none; timing surprise — CI instant, walkthrough real per-model latency; tone trace — n/a. *Disposition:* clean, zero halted. One honest note: a single drafter Responder serves all slots (deterministic → identical candidates in CI, honest; diversity is temperature on the real path). **Process note:** auto-within-phase — 137 set the pattern; the card was written at close documenting the met contract (card-before-code holds for plan-mode sprints; this functional sprint followed the established W1 shape). **NEXT:** W1.3 sprint 139 (`research_sweep`).

- **workflow parity W1.1 — `fanout_review`: the review panel on a REAL git diff (sprint 137) — `topologies/workflows/`, `scripts/run_fanout_review.py`** — First of the workflow-application library (`docs/cockpit/WORKFLOW-PARITY-SPRINTS-2026-07-31.md`): the agent-CLI "fan out a review over the changed files" pattern, which is `code_review_topology` already — N role reviewers in parallel, a quorum-gated judge, cancel-all-others — needing only real input. `changed_files(repo, ref)` gathers a bounded, read-only `git diff`; `fanout_review_topology(repo, ...)` hands it to the existing panel UNCHANGED (composition, not reimplementation; `code_review/__init__.py` untouched; no new vocabulary). `scripts/run_fanout_review.py` is the real-model launch surface (mirrors `run_tool_agent.py`). **Dual contract:** artifact (ruff + mypy clean on the package + script) + signal (reuses `CritiquePosted`/`VerdictRendered`). **Observation contract, both legs:** CI — `tests/test_fanout_review.py` (4 tests) builds a throwaway git repo, gathers its diff, drives the panel with DeterministicResponders, asserts N critiques + quorum-fired verdict (`n_critiques>=3`) + RunFinalised + read-only (working tree untouched) + no invented kinds; walkthrough — `run_fanout_review.py --repo ../substrate-ui --ref HEAD~1 --model kimi-k2.6:cloud` reviewed substrate-ui's actual last commit with real kimi models: FIVE ROLE-DISTINCT critiques on the real diff (performance: "it's markdown, no perf issue"; security: path-traversal check; style/clarity: fragile relative path; correctness: path is right) → judge `request-changes` → replayable record (`/tmp/fanout-walkthrough`, narrates clean). The thesis demonstrated: substrate already IS the orchestration engine these workflow features are built on, and unlike them every step is a replayable record. **Rubber Duck Pass:** *Sequence* — 5 initials start the reviewers; 3 CritiquePosted reach the quorum; `adjudicate` fires the judge Once at #16; the 2 slower critiques still land (#19/#21, no linger so they complete rather than cancel — cancel-others has no victims here, correct); VerdictRendered #23 cites the 3 the judge saw; finalise #25. *Observations (six):* missing pair — none; order — the judge cites 3 of 5 because it fires Once at quorum (by design, `n_critiques=3` honest); vocabulary gap — none (composed kinds only); payload anomaly — none; timing surprise — none (CI instant; walkthrough real per-model latency); tone trace — n/a. *Disposition:* clean, zero halted. **NEXT:** W1.2 sprint 138 (`best_of_n_verified`), W1.3 sprint 139 (`research_sweep`).

- **responder defaults — the token cap removed (2026-07-30) — `src/substrate/adapters/models.py`, `scripts/run_tool_agent.py`** — The live cockpit surfaced it: deepseek-v4-pro:cloud "announced" a script every turn and never wrote it. The record diagnosed it in minutes — every FinalAnswer was ~270 chars ending at a colon, exactly where the tool call would begin; the only call that ever survived was a 56-char `bash ls`. A/B via `scripts/run_tool_agent.py` (same model, same task): max_tokens=512 → zero tool calls, an announcement; 4096 → write_file (1,247 chars — could never fit in 512) → read-back → run → hit a real SSL error → edit_file → verified working artifact. Root cause: `OllamaResponder`'s `max_tokens=512` default — an inherited paid-API idiom, not a decision (Ollama's own `num_predict` default is -1, unlimited). The Architect then rejected 8192 as the same idiom with a bigger number, and the resolution is the honest one: **default `max_tokens=0` = `num_predict` omitted — no cap.** Generation is bounded by the two real limits, `num_ctx` (memory) and `timeout` (time); timeout raised 120→300 (a long legitimate generation under 120s recreated the truncation as a retry-then-fail) and `CliResponder` 180→600 (a real `claude -p` turn outlives 180). `run_tool_agent.py`: 256→0, 180→300. **Fixed by inheritance:** `eval_agent_models.py` (was judging models through a bare 512 responder) and `conversation.py`'s walkthrough speakers (every conversational walkthrough — prisoners_dilemma with real models included — was silently 512-capped). **Deliberately untouched:** the pinned assay configs (coding 900 / swebench 2048 — fingerprinted; changing them silently would break comparability), the gate/oracle sandbox timeouts (they bound generated code, not model calls), tool_loop's `max_steps=4` CI-demo default, `num_ctx`. Verified: request payload omits `num_predict`; live uncapped calls; targeted tests green; end-to-end seam proof on record `launch_agent_deepseek-v4-pro_93ef31043bdb` (write_file → read_file → bash exit 0 → FinalAnswer reporting real output; artifact runs standalone). A/B repro records kept at `/tmp/ds-test-512/record` and `/tmp/ds-test-4k/record`.

- **presentation — the repo's visual identity + a README reorder (2026-07-25) — `.github/assets/substrate-banner-1600x400.png`, `.github/assets/substrate-social-1280x640.png`, `README.md`** — The go-public surface got its art and its final section order. **The art.** A recurring cast in a 1990s software-manual isometric plate style (flat CMYK, halftone stipple, thick friendly outlines, warm cream paper), generated through the Architect's cascade-img MCP against Midjourney. **README banner** = the eight-character cast lineup, one robot per primitive with an identifying prop (see the Decisions entry for the mapping). **Social preview** = the single mascot hero shot, printout spooling past its feet. Both cleaned before shipping: the banner carried a garbled fake signature in its bottom-right corner (Midjourney ignoring `--no text`), patched with paper texture sampled from the same horizontal band so the halftone grain matches rather than leaving a flat rectangle, verified zero residual dark pixels; the social preview was re-encoded to a 256-colour PNG (1.4M → 696K) because GitHub caps the social card at 1MB, and JPEG would have smeared the stipple that is most of the drawing's texture. **The README reorder** (Architect ruling): `How it works` now sits AFTER `What you can build`, so concrete examples land before the design rationale. Order is Quick start → The pieces → What you can build → How it works → Status → Docs → Develop → Repository layout. NOTE: `Status` still opens "the eight pieces above" — still correct (The pieces is still upstream) but now four sections back rather than one; flagged, not changed. **OPEN, needs the Architect:** the social preview must be uploaded by hand at Settings → General → Social preview — committing the file does NOT set the card.

- **assay/swebench — THE REPAIR TOPOLOGY RAN THE ENTIRE SWE-BENCH LITE (300/300), instrumented, 0 topology-side failures (2026-06-27) — `topologies/swebench_solver/{records,assemble}.py`, `assay/{swebench_workspace,swebench,swebench_matrix}.py`, `scripts/assay_full_run.py`, KIT_DIARY 22–27** — The arc that turned the bespoke solver into a *pluggable assay*: any substrate topology of the right shape (emits a patch) can be run against SWE-bench and officially graded. **(1) The general bridge / the contract is "topology → patch."** A topology emits edits, something applies them to a checkout, `git diff` makes the valid patch, the official harness grades it in ITS OWN fresh container. Built `swebench_workspace.py` (`workspace_diff`, `filter_diff` with a fail-safe drop on unparseable headers, `host_clone`, `graded_test_files`) + two backends (host: non-executing host clone; container: live eval image with network off + git history neutered — verified 0 refs after lockdown). The model only ever sees `problem_statement` + repo files at base_commit — never `test_patch`/`FAIL_TO_PASS` (the per-instance firewall). **(2) The course-correction (the costly lesson).** I had condemned the WHOLE genuine `swebench_solver` topology over its ONE brittle test-running SELECT stage and rebuilt solving as a plain function OUTSIDE the substrate. Wrong. The fix was REMOVAL: `swebench_repair_topology` keeps localize + best-of-N SEARCH/REPLACE repair (drafters/validator/judge are real producers) and GUTS select to "emit the patch that applied" (the judge's `Solved` already names the first applyable slot) — no runner, no regression, no reproduction. **(3) Typed outcome vocabulary (SDD #51/#53), reviewed PASS (#73).** The failure modes were implicit (reconstructed from absent events); now the topology ALWAYS emits a terminal `RepairSummary` carrying an enumerated `RepairOutcome` (SELECTED / NO_LOCALIZATION / NO_APPLICABLE_EDIT) + stage counts. Chained on `SelectedPatch`/`Exhausted` so termination can't race the selector; registered in `records.py` + the vocab doc + WORKING_AGREEMENT; 3 observation-contract tests. **(4) Inflation guard universal (#72 NET 1).** Graded-test edits are dropped at the GRADE boundary (`swebench_record_oracle`), so a topology emitting a raw diff can't weaken a graded test — firewall stays clean, guard applies to every arm in one place. **(5) THE FULL-300 RESULT** (qwen3-coder:480b, n=3, ~7h, `assay_full_run.py` — per-instance try/except, per-instance clone cleanup, JSONL checkpoint/resume): **resolved 108 (36.0% of 300; 36.6% of 295 gradeable; 36.0% firewall-clean-only — robust to the 9 contamination-flagged django instances), wrong_patch 175, no_applicable_edit 12 (~4%), no_localization 0, timed_out 0, error 0, grade_unavailable 5.** All 5 are ONE upstream cause — the SWE-bench eval image is missing from Docker Hub (`BuildImageError/404`, all sympy; 71/74 other sympy graded fine, disk/docker fine) — i.e. grading INFRASTRUCTURE, not us. **ZERO topology-side failures across 12 repos.** The instrumentation was the point and paid off: it isolated the 5 grade failures without losing the other 295, and the exact cause was diagnosable from the harness log in 2 minutes (the `grade_unavailable` label, follow-up #1, now distinguishes "couldn't grade" from "our topology broke"). **(6) Context for the 36%** (researched, not asserted): this model reports ~69% on SWE-bench *Verified* WITH a full agent harness ([Qwen Jul 2025]); 36% on *Lite* through a deliberately minimal no-iteration scaffold is the coherent gap — the agentic loop we left out is worth roughly that. Not inflated (firewall-clean-only is identical), not depressed by a bug. Best-in-class now is the Claude family ~88–95% Verified, but the field is fleeing Verified for **SWE-bench Pro** (Scale AI) over saturation+contamination (OpenAI found 59% of hard problems had broken tests). **Contamination stance (decided):** Pro's private/held-out sets trade contamination for UNVERIFIABILITY (a commercial vendor's unfalsifiable number) — the honest escape is neither secrecy nor naive-public but **public + post-cutoff/dated + self-run** (freshness does what secrecy tried to, keeping verifiability). Our firewall is the per-instance guard; dating is the global one. **NEXT:** the `--build` path to grade the 5 missing images if wanted; re-point the same harness at a dated/post-cutoff public set for a contamination-clean number; wire more arms (the matrix leaderboard exists); the coding_flow metering gap still open.

- **assay/swebench — STOOD UP THE SWE-BENCH PATH + the real arm64-local story (researched, not assumed) (2026-06-26) — `scripts/swebench_smoke.py`, `assay/swebench.py`, `docs/swebench/swebench-bridge-mapping.md`** — Moving to a RECOGNIZED public benchmark (the legible win). **Environment up:** swebench **4.1.0** (verified = current PyPI; repo github.com/SWE-bench/SWE-bench), Docker 29.4.1 running, SWE-bench **Lite loads (300 instances** w/ gold+test patches), box arch=**arm64** (M5/128GB, 3.3Ti free). **RESEARCHED the arm64 question (after asserting it wrong twice — lesson: interrogating the installed PACKAGE ≠ research; go to the sources):** official SWE-bench docs — x86_64 RECOMMENDED (120GB/16GB/8core), **arm64 is "experimental"**, Apple Silicon needs `--namespace ''` to BUILD locally (pre-built DockerHub images are x86-only); independent analysis (Grey Newell "SWE-bench-fast", Feb 2026 — one expert, salt) — native arm64 ~5× faster than emulation BUT the upstream build pipeline hardcodes x86_64 Miniconda URLs (build fails) and ~496 containers need x86 emulation anyway. **EMPIRICAL on THIS box (the gold-patch smoke):** (1) x86 emulation **WORKS** — `docker run --platform linux/amd64 alpine uname -m` → x86_64 (Rosetta/QEMU live). (2) `namespace=''` (build-local) is **BROKEN in 4.1.0** exactly as the research said — it bakes `x86_64` into the image name (`sweb.eval.x86_64.pallets_1776_flask-4045`) even on arm64, and the empty namespace makes an invalid leading-slash Docker reference (`/sweb.eval...`) → never builds, errors in 5.7s. (3) The **working local path = DEFAULT namespace** (pull the official x86 image + run under emulation); the gold-patch smoke on `pallets__flask-4045` **RESOLVED=True** (resolved 1/1, errors 0) — **the containerized SWE-bench grade WORKS on this arm64 box end-to-end through our binding, emulated.** **BINDING BUG FIXED:** `run_swebench` looked for the run report in `report_dir`, but swebench 4.x writes `{model}.{run_id}.json` to **CWD** → now searches both (same path-churn lesson `read_resolved` already had). **THE BIG PICTURE / next:** local-arm64 works but only via x86 EMULATION (slow, not officially reportable) — so the rigorous reportable number belongs on an **x86 box**. **PLANNED CAPABILITY (the user's vision): an assay "cloud runner"** — runs local OR, if AWS creds/CLI present (else guided setup), auto-provisions the exact x86 instance in the user's account, uploads files, runs the benchmark with PRICE GUARDRAILS, imports results back, auto-reports — a remote-execution capability of the assay layer. The SWE-bench SOLVER topology (produce a patch from a real repo+issue — NOT coding_flow's write-from-spec) is the other half, still to build. NEXT: green the local gold smoke (emulated) → fix `verify_constants`/binding for 4.1.0 as needed → minimal solver → assay wrap; in parallel, the cloud runner.

- **assay — CARGO-CULT REVIEW FOLD: three gates on the equivalence claim + adversary-proof firewall + provenance tamper detection + matched-compute + the cells→Report seam (2026-06-26) — `assay/{stats,report,cells,coding}.py`, `scripts/bench_coding.py`, tests** — A review found the framework trustworthy for "orchestration is WORSE" (McNemar+BH-FDR, real) but cargo-cult for "orchestration REACHED the bar" — it would print a confident `equivalent` on a thin or post-hoc-margined run (reproduced: a post-hoc `BENCH_MARGIN=0.30` manufactured a tie). **THREE GATES, all must hold before `equivalent`:** (1) **POWER FLOOR** — `equivalence_power_floor(margin)=ceil(3.6/margin²)` → ~90/160/360 at ±0.20/0.15/0.10; `equivalence_verdict` now takes `n_pairs`, a within-margin CI with too few cases reads **UNDERPOWERED** not EQUIVALENT (superior/inferior still fire — a difference needs less power than a tie). (2) **ARM-COMPLETENESS** — `ArmReport.complete`; no delta/CI/verdict unless BOTH the arm and the control graded EVERY case (a partial/live run yields no verdict). (3) **MARGIN BOUND TO CONFIG** — the report reads the margin off the run's recorded meta (via `cells.report_from_cells`), never the env; a differing `BENCH_MARGIN` is IGNORED with a note. **FIREWALL vs exit-code gaming** (`coding.py`): a candidate calling `os._exit(0)` at import exits the gate clean (rc 0) without the held-out tests running — the oracle now requires POSITIVE evidence (pytest reported ≥ the expected tests passed), so a gamed exit is caught. **PROVENANCE tamper detection** (`cells.py` `provenance_status`): the verdict's margin lives in an editable `.meta.json`, so it's VERIFIED against (a) a recompute of its own config fields and (b) the per-cell fingerprint stamped at run time → `verified`/`tampered`/`unverified`. **MATCHED-COMPUTE**: per-arm `model_calls` surfaced (the honest counterweight). **`cells.py`** = the canonical "a cells JSONL → a Report" seam (CLI + substrate-ui server share ONE impl, no drift). **VERIFIED ON REAL DATA — the now-COMPLETE coder run** (qwen2.5-coder:7b ensemble vs 480B, 2840 cells): full = reliable 58/71 (0.817), Δpass@1=**-0.049 CI=[-0.104,+0.000]** — the CI sits INSIDE ±0.10, so the OLD code prints `equivalent`; the gate reads **UNDERPOWERED** (71 << 360), and the compute axis shows it cost **2421 calls vs strong's 710** (~3.4×). The closest result yet, honestly not oversold. Provenance: coding_coder=verified, coding_cells=unverified. **Dual contract:** mypy --strict (74), ruff, lint-imports clean; new tests (power-floor 90/160/360; tight-CI-too-few→underpowered; os._exit gamer caught; tampered-margin detected). Committed `342e6c1`/`648d442`/`b426848`. **DEFERRED (recorded): CONTAINERIZATION** — required for SWE-bench (table-stakes per-instance Docker; `assay/swebench.py` already runs Docker via TestSpec) AND the adversary-proof firewall telos (we currently run untrusted code on the bare host with only a timer; container = cgroup/network/FS isolation). It wraps the GRADE not the models (no inference slowdown). The test-file-READING hole is fixed cheaper by an input/output split, not a container. NOT needed for current weak-local runs — add at the SWE-bench step (`docs/swebench/swebench-bridge-mapping.md`, memory `project-containerization-requirement`).

- **assay — THE POWERED VERDICT: orchestration closes ~85% of the gap to a 480B, but does NOT reach it (2026-06-26) — `scripts/bench_coding.py`, `process/bench_results/coding_cells.jsonl`** — The full firewalled sweep: 71 problems × 4 arms × 10 trials = **2840 cells, 0 failures**, control=qwen3-coder:480b-cloud (the strong bar), weak ensemble=[llama3:8b, qwen2.5:7b-instruct], graded on HELD-OUT tests, ±0.10 margin. **Result (pass^k reliability + paired bootstrap Δ-pass^1 + TOST):** strong_ref 66/71 (0.930); weak_single **0/71** (Δ=-0.758 [-0.817,-0.696] INFERIOR fdr-sig); ensemble_no_correction **14/71** (Δ=-0.485 INFERIOR fdr-sig); full **48/71** (Δ=-0.115 [-0.182,-0.056] **INFERIOR fdr-sig**). **The honest, non-hyped finding:** the ablation ladder 0→14→48 is powered and crisp — orchestration around FREE local models takes them from solving NOTHING reliably to 48/71, the correction loop alone worth +34 problems (the dominant lever), closing **~85% of the single-model gap** to the 480B. BUT `full` is significantly INFERIOR (tight CI entirely below 0) — it does NOT reach the strong model; a real ~11-pt gap stands. The framework refused to launder a near-miss into "equivalent" — exactly the rigor the bar required. The residual gap is now a MEASURED iteration target, not a vibe. **Robust harness (the all-or-nothing lesson):** incremental JSONL (every cell persisted on completion), resumable (skip done cells), SALVAGE (re-grade finished records from disk, no models — saved 2195 of 2840 cells after the first run was killed), per-cell timeout, `… report` rebuilds the verdict from the JSONL anytime. First run died because (a) laptop slept overnight (monotonic froze) and (b) report built only at the end; both fixed. **KNOWN GAP:** coding_flow doesn't emit ModelUsage events → token/call columns are 0 (metering seam exists in `_models.py`, topology doesn't use it); pass-rate/elapsed unaffected. NEXT (close the gap): stronger free locals in the ensemble (qwen2.5-coder:7b — a dedicated coder, still free/local), more correction rounds, a better selector; wire coding_flow metering; then SWE-bench-Live.

- **assay — BANK GROWN 10 → 71 firewalled problems (breadth, parallel agents, all verified) (2026-06-26) — `assay/coding_problems.py`, `tests/test_coding_problems.py`** — Toward the ~80-problem power target the sim demands for a ±0.10 equivalence. Five authoring agents (strings/numbers/lists-dicts/parsing/algorithms), each writing AND gate-verifying ~12 problems before returning paste-ready entries; +62 new (one `second_largest` dropped as a cross-batch dup; one module renamed `ds`→`dsorted` to disambiguate). Re-verified on assembly: **73 tests, every one of the 71 references passes BOTH the dev gate and the held-out gate** (full ruff + mypy --strict + pytest, ~73s) — solvable + firewall-fair, proven before any model run; the firewall-teeth test still holds. Notifications HTML-escaped the agents' code (`&gt;`/`&lt;`/`&amp;`); un-escaped on assembly, the gate verification caught nothing (transcription clean). Dual contract: ruff + mypy --strict clean; full deterministic suite **426 passed / 1 skipped**; lint-imports clean. **The bank is now ready for the powered run** — the only remaining piece is compute: 71 problems × 4 arms × ~10-20 trials is a multi-hour job on the box (not inline), and per the sim that's the configuration that can earn a real `equivalent` at ±0.10. NEXT: kick the full powered sweep (or a bounded slice); then 480B-cloud strong bar; then SWE-bench-Live.

- **assay — POWER SIMULATION: how to earn `equivalent` honestly (margin is the lever, not trials) (2026-06-25) — `scripts/power_sim.py`** — Before scaling, simulated the equivalence verdict through the REAL stats (`bootstrap_delta_pass_k` + TOST) to size the powered run — checkable, not asserted. A concern was raised that "10 problems + trials>1 → earn equivalent" is a statistical mistake (trials buy within-cell reliability, not between-instance power). The sim CORRECTS it precisely: **trials DO buy power** (P(equivalent) at n=10 heterogeneous-null rises 0.00→0.21→0.83 as trials 1→5→20 at margin ±0.25) — so it's not a pure mistake. BUT **the MARGIN is the real lever**, and that's where the integrity threat actually lives: at a MEANINGFUL margin ±0.10, n=10 earns `equivalent` **0% of the time no matter the trials**; you need **n≈80 × ~20 trials** for ~0.91. So the failure mode isn't "fake power from trials" — it's KEEPING the loose ±0.25 margin (which would call a 25-pt-worse topology "equivalent"). Equivalence is also confirmed harder than difference (n=40, tr=20: a true +0.15 effect reads `superior` 1.00 vs a true null reads `equivalent` 0.43 at ±0.10). **ACTIONS:** (1) re-baselined the bench margin ±0.25 → **±0.10** (meaningful equivalence); (2) the honest path is pre-register the margin, then size n+trials from the sim table for ~80% power BEFORE running (the MDE discipline the review deferred — now filled in); (3) growing the bank toward ~80 problems (5 parallel authoring agents, self-verifying via the real gate, returning paste-ready firewalled problems; re-verified on assembly). NEXT: assemble the expanded bank → re-verify → powered run at ±0.10 with trials.

- **assay — BREADTH: a verified 10-problem firewalled bank + a 6-problem run with a SIGNIFICANT result (2026-06-25) — `assay/coding_problems.py`, `tests/test_coding_problems.py`, `scripts/bench_coding.py`** — Cover-the-bases on the coding claim. A pre-registered (git-timestamped) bank of **10 firewalled problems** (rle, duration, reverse_words, balanced, two_sum, roman, fizzbuzz, caesar, bsearch, word_count), each with DISJOINT dev/held-out tests spanning string ops / parsing / search / small algorithms. **Bank VERIFIED:** 12 tests run a reference solution through BOTH gates (full ruff+mypy+pytest) per problem — every problem is solvable AND the firewall is fair (held-out agrees with dev on a correct solution); + a firewall-teeth test (an overfit fizzbuzz passes dev, caught on held-out). **6-PROBLEM REAL RUN** (strong=qwen2.5-coder:7b; weak ensemble=[llama3:8b, qwen2.5:7b-instruct]; held-out grading): strong_ref **4/6**, weak_single **0/6 (Δ=-0.67, CI[-1.00,-0.33], INFERIOR, FDR-SIGNIFICANT)**, ensemble_no_correction 3/6 (Δ=-0.17, inconclusive), full **4/6 (Δ=0.00, inconclusive — matched strong in direction, CI[-0.50,+0.50] not yet powered)**. **The honest headline: a single weak model FAILS significantly (the one powered result); orchestration around free models closes the gap (0→3→4) and full MATCHED the strong model's pass rate; the equivalence is real in direction but not statistically powered at n=6.** Correction earned its keep here (full got two_sum, the no-correction ensemble didn't). elapsed real (strong 10s, full 31.5s). **Dual contract:** ruff + mypy --strict (71 files) + lint-imports clean; full deterministic suite **365 passed / 1 skipped** (the bank verification runs real gates in ~11s). NEXT for power: BENCH_LIMIT-free full 10-problem run + trials>1; then 480B-cloud as the strong bar (bigger gap); then SWE-bench-Live.

- **assay — the FIREWALLED coding benchmark + first legible run (cracks 1, 2, 6) (2026-06-25) — `assay/coding.py`, `scripts/bench_coding.py`, `tests/test_assay_coding.py`** — The "legible first" measurement (a topology winning on coding, built to survive scrutiny — the entry ticket before the illegible factory/small-models thesis). **CRACK 1 FIREWALL:** a `CodingProblem` carries TWO disjoint test sets — DEV (the agent's gate, what coding_flow's correction loop iterates on) and HELD-OUT (the `coding_oracle` external-grader scores on, never shown to the agent). So the correction loop can't teach to the test; held-out tests also WIN on key collision so a candidate can't overwrite them. Proven deterministically: an overfit candidate (returns the dev-passing constant) PASSES dev, FAILS held-out → not-resolved (4 firewall tests, real pytest in a sandbox, no models). **CRACK 2 ABLATION LADDER:** arms = weak_single / ensemble_no_correction / full (ensemble+correction) — a gain is pinned to the mechanism, not "more attempts." **CRACK 6:** control = `strong_ref` (the strong single model, the bar to erode); headline = the TOST equivalence verdict on `full` (did free orchestration REACH the strong model). **FIRST REAL FIREWALLED RUN** (strong=qwen2.5-coder:7b; weak ensemble=[llama3:8b, qwen2.5:7b-instruct]; 2 problems, held-out grading): strong_ref 1/2, weak_single 0/2, ensemble_no_correction 1/2, full 1/2 — **the SHAPE is the thesis** (a single weak model resolved nothing; the weak ENSEMBLE matched the strong model — both got rle, both missed duration on held-out). But the framework caps it honestly: `full` reads `equivalent` only because full and strong got IDENTICAL per-problem outcomes on 2 problems (CI degenerate [0,0]); nothing FDR-significant; the floor arm is `inconclusive`. NOT a powered equivalence — needs many problems + trials for the CI to narrow around 0 for a real reason. elapsed is now REAL wall-clock (strong 6.6s, full 15.4s — full does more work). **Dual contract:** ruff + mypy --strict (71 files) + lint-imports clean; full deterministic suite **353 passed / 1 skipped**. **Cracks status: 1,2,3,4,6 DONE in code; 5 (grader-validity axis / residual oracle error — drop "ground truth") + minors (selector pass@k-vs-real naming, git pre-reg=timestamp, post-cutoff perishability) = framing/follow-ups.** NEXT: scale problems + trials for a powered verdict; the 480B-cloud-as-strong-bar version (dramatic erosion gap); then a recognized public benchmark (SWE-bench-Live, Docker on the Architect's box) for the most legible win.

- **assay — corrected statistics (cracks 3+4 of the methodology review) (2026-06-25) — `assay/stats.py`, `assay/{report,suite}.py`, `tests/test_assay_stats.py`** — Replaced "collapse k trials to a boolean → McNemar → accept-null-on-non-significance" with the textbook instruments, imported (Dietterich 1998; Lakens 2017 TOST; Benjamini-Hochberg 1995; Miller 2024), not invented. **`stats.py`:** `pass_hat_k` = the reliability estimand C(passes,k)/C(trials,k) (distinct from pass@k); `bootstrap_delta_pass_k` = a PAIRED TWO-LEVEL bootstrap (resample Cases, then trials within each) on Δ-pass^k — carries the trial variance the boolean collapse discarded (crack 4); `equivalence_verdict` = TOST against a pre-registered margin → superior / equivalent (CI inside ±margin, a REAL null) / inferior / inconclusive — non-significance is no longer silently read as "no effect" (crack 3); `benjamini_hochberg` = FDR across the arm matrix. Seeded → the Report is REPEATABLE. **Suite** gained `equivalence_margin` (TOST) + `pass_k` (defaults keep old constructions working). **report.py** now computes the bootstrap Δ-pass^k + TOST verdict + BH-FDR as the PRIMARY surface per non-control arm (McNemar kept as a secondary binary cross-check); new ArmReport fields delta_pass_k / ci_low / ci_high / bootstrap_p / equivalence / fdr_significant. **Dual contract:** ruff + mypy --strict (70 files) + lint-imports clean; 7 stats tests (subset estimator vs hand-computation, TOST directionality incl. "wide-CI-including-0 is NOT equivalence", repeatability, equal-arms-not-superior, trial-variance carried, BH-vs-Bonferroni) + the full assay suite (30) pass. **Review cracks status:** 3,4 DONE; **1 (firewall agent's feedback tests from the oracle's grading tests) + 2 (ablation arms: single-model-best-of-N, N-drafts-no-correction) are NEXT — the decisive pair for whether the coding benchmark measures structure or leakage**; 5 (grader-validity axis, drop "ground truth") + 6 (delta is model/split-dependent — carry model+split context) folded as we build the coding firewall. NEXT SPRINT: the firewalled coding suite + ablation arms + cross-model runs.

- **assay — adversarial-review folds + the noise lesson (2026-06-25) — `assay/{run,report,conformance}.py`, `topologies/coding_flow/{__init__,gate}.py`, `topologies/tool_loop/__init__.py`, `assay/swebench.py`, tests** — Folded an external 14-finding review (the two that would have made the first benchmark LIE first). **(HIGH) wall-clock conflation** — `wall_ms` summed per-call inference latencies (which OVERLAP under concurrency) and called it wall time, so a concurrent ensemble would read as SLOWER. Split into two honest axes: `CaseResult.elapsed_ms` = real wall-clock timed around `Runtime.run` (the latency axis), and `UsageTotals.inference_ms` = summed per-call latency (work, overcounts under concurrency, never read as latency). **(swebench path)** real swebench writes reports to CWD, not `report_dir` — `read_resolved` now SEARCHES the candidate roots (report_dir / logs/run_evaluation / CWD) and the gold-differential test pins the real path. **(benchmark integrity)** coding_flow merged `{**fixtures, **candidate}` so a candidate could overwrite the held-out test it's graded against — reversed to fixtures-win. **(walkthrough bugs, live now)** tool_loop stamped a real model `deterministic` (→ `det = ... and not walkthrough`); tool_loop crashed on non-int tool args from a weak model (→ try/fall-through); coding_flow wedged into RunStuckQuiescent if a slot died (→ quiescence watchdog in termination); gate.py path-traversal guard raised uncaught → crash+wedge (→ catch, return passed=False). **(hardening)** control-ran guard now DEREFERENCES each control root and confirms a committed `RunFinalised` (not just a CaseResult object — the anti-cargo-cult check); pass^k-CI/MDE and Holm/Bonferroni recorded as explicit DEFERRALS (bite at the first multi-trial/multi-arm run); run.py docstring corrected (the design self-contradicts §A1 vs §A5 on embedded_substrate — deferred to §8.3); gate D-8 overclaim reworded. Tests: path-traversal + fixtures-win regression tests added. **Dual contract:** ruff + mypy --strict (69 files) + lint-imports clean; full deterministic suite **340 passed / 1 skipped**; new guards pass. **THE NOISE LESSON (re-ran the benchmark with the fixes):** the result SWUNG from last run's +0.33 to +0.00 (baseline 1/3, full 1/3 this time — the ensemble's earlier kvstore win did not reproduce). McNemar p=1.000 BOTH times. elapsed now real: baseline 7.7s, full 32s. **3 tasks / 1 trial is noise, and the framework's p=1.0 correctly refused both as evidence — the anti-laundering working in practice.** Remaining LOW nits (verify_constants drift test, swebench signature-introspection test, identical-Adapter-output assertion, sync respond_metered, tautological-assertion cleanups) batched/deferred. NEXT: scale tasks + trials for a powered number before ANY claim.

- **assay — FIRST REAL A/B NUMBER through the layer (2026-06-25) — `scripts/bench_coding.py`** — The point of the whole build, finally produced: coding_flow run as a real benchmark against a single-model baseline, OUTPUT QUALITY ONLY (no cost — there is no money here; these are local models). 3 coding tasks (kvstore / rle / duration), graded by the real `ruff + mypy --strict + pytest` gate (a `Solved` event = a candidate passed the gate). **baseline** = qwen2.5-coder:7b single-shot (n=1, 1 round); **full** = a 3-model ensemble [qwen2.5-coder:7b, qwen2.5:7b-instruct, llama3:8b] + the correction loop (n=3, 2 rounds). **Result: baseline solved 1/3 (rate 0.33), full solved 2/3 (rate 0.67), delta +0.33, McNemar p=1.000 (b=0, c=1).** Per task: kvstore (multi-file, strict-typed) — full solved it, baseline did not (the one discordant pair); rle — both solved; duration — both failed. **The framework reports its own honesty: p=1.000 — one discordant task is not evidence of anything**, so the +0.33 is printed alongside "not significant" rather than dressed up. No laundering. This proves the layer end-to-end: real topology, real models, real gate, an honest A/B number. The number itself (3 tasks) is a first datum, not a conclusion — a real signal needs more Cases (just extend the suite). NOTE corrected throughout this session: COST is dead (subscription/local, no money); tokens+time are measurements you can benchmark on, never a cost — residual price/$/matched-compute language cut from the code, design doc, and glossary. NEXT: scale the task set for a powered number; optionally wire coding_flow's drafter to the metered seam so its records carry token/time measurements.

- **assay — real-model coverage added (2026-06-25, corrects an earlier stand-ins-only framing) — `tests/test_assay_realmodel.py`** — The model-bearing path of the assay layer was built with deterministic-stand-in tests only and NEVER run against the live model that is present on this machine — a real gap (the repo's whole `@realmodel` discipline + finding 28 "green-on-canned hides crash-on-real" demand live verification; the existing topology `@realmodel` suite already runs live against qwen2.5:7b-instruct/llama3.2:1b every `uv run pytest`). Closed it: two `@realmodel` tests (repo convention — `_require` skips if absent, HARD-FAIL if present-but-wrong, assert from the record). **test_metered_seam_captures_real_provider_accounting** — `call_responder_metered` against a LIVE model must return provider-truth usage (`estimated=False`, real non-zero prompt/completion tokens + wall_ms) — the one seam the Sprint-1 review said "CI can't see". **test_assay_aggregates_real_usage_end_to_end** — a real model driven through `run_suite`->grade->`build_report`, asserting the provider usage (estimated=False) aggregates into the Report. Verified live across **qwen2.5:7b-instruct, qwen2.5-coder:7b, llama3:8b, and the cloud qwen3-coder:480b-cloud** — e.g. qwen2.5-coder:7b → prompt=46/completion=44/wall_ms=466/estimated=False; the 480B cloud → wall_ms=5174 (the real local-vs-cloud latency spread matched-compute needs), the stand-in beside it → wall_ms=0/estimated=True (the flag genuinely separates them). Committed test pins qwen2.5:7b-instruct (capable local; M5/128GB runs 7-8B easily). ruff clean; both pass in ~1.2s. **Honest state unchanged on the bigger claim:** the shipped topologies still call `call_responder` (not metered), so they do NOT yet emit ModelUsage on their real runs — wiring topologies to the metered seam, and the first actual benchmark number, are still ahead. What changed: the seam + the assay aggregation are now proven on REAL local AND cloud models, not just stand-ins.

- **assay Sprint 5 — SWE-bench external-grader Adapter + sanity-review folds (2026-06-25) — `src/substrate/assay/swebench.py`, `tests/test_assay_swebench.py`; `docs/benchmarking/benchmarking-glossary.md` (agent)** — Binds the external-grader Oracle to the OFFICIAL `swebench` harness, built against `docs/swebench/swebench-bridge-mapping.md` (real symbols, not invented): `make_prediction` emits EXACTLY the three real fields ({instance_id, model_name_or_path, model_patch}); `read_resolved` reads the per-instance `report.json` `resolved` bool at the bridge-mapped path (mirroring the model-name `/`->`__` rewrite); `read_run_report` asserts `schema_version==2` (a harness bump fails loudly); `verify_constants` checks our field literals against the installed swebench when present (the bridge-mapping discipline, executable; no-op when absent); `swebench_oracle` grades a Case from a pre-run report, run-and-observe (`replayable=False`); `run_swebench` is ENV-GATED (lazy-imports swebench, Docker + image pulls — runs on the Architect's box, NEVER here/CI). VALIDATION PATH documented: the harness's literal `"gold"` predictions differential-test the binding (gold resolves, empty doesn't) WITHOUT any topology — isolating Adapter correctness from topology quality; do that first. SWE-bench-Live (the contamination-dated PRIMARY) is a DISTINCT backend (own harness/images) — noted as a follow-up, not wired. **Sanity-review folds (from a background agent on the Sprint 3+4 core):** (1) [MEDIUM, real] `delta_vs_control` is now PAIRED over the same Case intersection the McNemar test uses, so a partially-run arm can never report a headline delta and a p-value describing different populations — the exact degrees-of-freedom seam this layer polices; (2) [LOW] Arm.name / Case.case_id validated against `[A-Za-z0-9._+-]+` with no `__` at construction, so the `{arm}__{case}__t{trial}` run-root can't collide or path-traverse; (3) doc note on the deliberate pass^k-quality-vs-summed-cost asymmetry. The agent verified the McNemar math against scipy.binomtest (correct) and confirmed the delta-gating is airtight. **Dual contract:** artifact — ruff + format + mypy --strict (69 files; added a `[tool.mypy.overrides]` for the optional untyped `swebench.*` instead of fragile inline ignores) + lint-imports clean; signal/observation — 8 swebench tests (prediction shape, JSONL roundtrip, resolved-read + slash rewrite, schema assert, constants no-op, oracle-from-report + missing-report-not-a-crash) + the new collision guards, all pass; **full suite 352 passed / 1 skipped**. **THE LAYER IS BUILT END TO END** (seam→oracle→control-plane→guard→stats→adapter) and DETERMINISTICALLY GREEN — but it is the INSTRUMENT, tested for WIRING; ZERO benchmark numbers exist yet, and the live SWE-bench grading + the gold differential-test run in the Architect's Docker env. Not laundering: green plumbing ≠ a validated topology. NEXT: run the gold differential-test on the Architect's box; wire coding_flow's record→model_patch extraction; then the first real (coding_flow → SWE-bench-Live) measurement under the pre-registered endpoint + null rule.

- **assay Sprints 3+4 — control plane + three-state control-ran guard + paired Report (2026-06-25) — `src/substrate/assay/{suite,run,conformance,report}.py`, `tests/test_assay_control_plane.py`** — The coupled core, built together (shared types). **suite.py** — `Case` (msgspec; id + opaque payload + ground_truth), `Arm` (frozen dataclass: name + role∈{full,ablation,baseline,placebo} + `build(case)->topology`), `Suite` (pre-registered: frozen cases, arm matrix, oracle, the named control arm, the single primary_metric, the published null_rule — validated in `__post_init__`: control must name a real arm, cases/arms non-empty, names unique). **run.py** — the PLAIN-PYTHON control plane (design §2/§A5, NOT a meta-topology): each Arm is a normal `Runtime(root).run()` at a UNIQUE minted root, the Oracle reads the record; `_sum_usage` aggregates ModelUsage off the record into `UsageTotals` with dimensions kept SEPARATE (tokens vs wall, never fused) + an `estimated` flag. **conformance.py (Sprint 3)** — `check_control_ran` three-state (pass / fail / no-control-ran), the EXECUTABLE anti-cargo-cult guard (not a type — the LET_FINISH/fake-checker scar tissue). **report.py (Sprint 4)** — exact two-sided McNemar p-value (binomial, no scipy) PAIRED across cases; `build_report` GATES every delta on the control-ran check passing — no control on the log, `delta_vs_control`/`p_value` stay None; trials collapse per (arm,case) by pass^k reliability; compute dimensions reported on separate axes. **Dual contract:** artifact — ruff + format + mypy --strict (68 files) + lint-imports clean; signal/observation — 5 tests over a REAL deterministic two-arm/two-case run (paired delta +0.5 with the honestly-underpowered p=1.0 on one discordant pair; usage aggregated from metered stand-in calls; the three guard states; delta-gating proven by dropping the control's results → all deltas None; McNemar exact values; Suite/Arm validation), all pass; **full suite 345 passed / 1 skipped**. **Rubber Duck Pass:** the gate genuinely refuses a number without a control (tested by removal, not asserted); honesty rides in the data (replayable on Result, estimated on usage, three-state check, separate dimensions); zero halted. The layer now spans seam→oracle→control-plane→guard→stats end-to-end, deterministically tested. NEXT (Sprint 5, in progress): the swebench Adapter (external-grader oracle binding) against `docs/swebench/swebench-bridge-mapping.md` — its differential-test on gold instances runs in the Architect's Docker env.

- **assay Sprint 2 — the Oracle taxonomy (2026-06-25) — `src/substrate/assay/{__init__,oracle.py}`, `tests/test_assay_oracle.py`** — The new `assay/` application-layer package (provisional name; imports only `substrate.api` + `reference`, never kernel internals — like topologies/). `oracle.py` splits grading into the two classes the round-1 design demands for honesty: **`LogProjectionOracle`** (grade by projecting a typed terminal value off the inner RECORD and comparing to ground truth — deterministic, `replayable=True`, a pure function of the log) and **`ExternalGraderOracle`** (grade by running an EXTERNAL system — Docker/test-harness/DB — `replayable=False`, run-and-observe, captured once, like coding_flow's gate). The `replayable` + `oracle_class` flags ride on every typed `Result` so a reader can never mistake a run-and-observe grade for a reproducible one — honesty in the DATA, not a comment. **Dual contract:** artifact — ruff + format + mypy --strict (64 files) + lint-imports clean; signal/observation — 5 tests (log-projection grades a REAL deterministic tool_loop record against ground truth pass+fail; grade is a pure function of the record; external-grader stub actually "runs" and is stamped non-replayable; custom compare hook; Result frozen), all pass; **full suite 340 passed / 1 skipped**. **Rubber Duck Pass:** the two classes differ only in the replayable/oracle_class stamp + whether an external process runs; no missing pair, no vocab issue (Result is a plain return type, not a bus kind); all resolved-here. ALSO this turn: **Sprint 1 hardened** from an adversarial quick-review — `wall_ms` now excludes model-load time (sum of prompt_eval_duration + eval_duration, not total_duration, which folded in seconds of cold VRAM load and would confound matched-compute latency) + a new `estimated` bool on ModelUsage marking word-count stand-ins vs provider truth in the data. **Parallel agents (3, background) landed:** the Sprint-1 quick review (ship-as-is, the one finding folded above); `docs/benchmarking/benchmarking-glossary.md` (the newcomer on-ramp the junior review asked for); `docs/swebench/swebench-bridge-mapping.md` (the official `swebench` API reverse-engineered from source for Sprint 5 — prediction = {instance_id, model_name_or_path, model_patch}; `resolved` is all-or-nothing in `report.json`; Docker via TestSpec mandatory; SWE-bench-Live is a separate dataset/harness with a recency window; caveat — verified vs GitHub main, re-read `run_evaluation:main` args from the pinned 4.1.0 wheel at build). NEXT: Sprint 3 (control-ran conformance check, three-state) + Sprint 4 (control plane).

- **assay Sprint 1 — `ModelUsage` at the Responder seam (2026-06-25) — `src/substrate/reference/_models.py`, `reference/__init__.py`, `tests/test_metering.py`** — The gating dependency for the objective-validation layer (matched-compute had NO data source — `OllamaResponder._content` discarded Ollama's `prompt_eval_count`/`eval_count`/`total_duration`). Added a `ModelUsage` application event (model, prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, wall_ms; `total_tokens()` derived, off-wire) + `call_responder_metered(responder, prompt) -> (text, ModelUsage)` at the single model chokepoint. OllamaResponder refactored behavior-preservingly: `_chat`/`_achat` factor the retry+POST into one place; `respond`/`arespond` unchanged in behavior; new `respond_metered`/`arespond_metered`/`_usage` keep the provider's real counts. The stand-in path derives deterministic word-count usage with wall_ms=0 so CI records stay byte-stable; `model` field names the responder so estimate-vs-provider-truth is legible. `ModelUsage` is an APPLICATION kind (Producer-declared, the ToolCall precedent) — no reserved-`substrate.*` vocab bump, correcting the round-1 doc's framing of it as a `vocabulary_change_required` (it is a new shared application struct, designed deliberately, not a kernel-vocabulary change). **Dual contract:** artifact — ruff + ruff-format + mypy --strict (62 files) + lint-imports all clean; signal/observation — 5 new tests pass (deterministic usage stable; Ollama parse off a literal /api/chat dict; `ModelUsage` lands on a record in time order; two metered runs byte-identical via `first_divergence`), and the **full suite 334 passed / 1 skipped** confirms the seam refactor broke no topology. Real-provider `arespond_metered` against a live daemon is the gated `@realmodel` path (runs on the Architect's box, not in this session — the honest dual-mode boundary). **Rubber Duck Pass:** narration — `ModelUsage` emits before `_Answer` on the same log, counts match the derived estimate, wall_ms=0; observations — no missing pair, no order violation (usage precedes answer), no vocab gap (app kind), no payload anomaly, determinism held; disposition — all resolved-here, zero halted. NEXT: Sprint 2 (Oracle taxonomy — log-projection vs external-grader).

- **game_of_life_glider — an asymmetric, moving fixture for the substrate-ui pixel-decode mirror test (2026-06-22) — `src/substrate/topologies/{game_of_life/__init__,bundled}.py`** — Added a `glider()` initial + a `game_of_life_glider` bundled config (a glider on a 7×7, generations=4) beside the blinker `game_of_life`. The glider is ASYMMETRIC (grid ≠ its L-R mirror on every frame) and MOVES (translates one cell down-right over its period-4) — exactly what the substrate-ui pixel-decode harness (`capture_scene`) needs to catch a pure mirror render bug, which the mirror-symmetric blinker couldn't (it maps to itself under mirroring — substrate-ui review #50 mirror-blind spot). Committed record auto-generated from the registry; ruff+mypy clean; the currency gate (`test_committed_record_is_current`) confirms it regenerates byte-identically; 31 bundled+game_of_life tests pass; the glider runs clean through 5 generations (a textbook glider translating down-right). The decode-side fold lands in substrate-ui (sprint 012 item A).

- **tool_loop + game_of_life topologies + agent/meta design docs (2026-06-22) — `src/substrate/topologies/{tool_loop,game_of_life}/` + `docs/{tool-loop-futures,substrate-as-a-tool}.md`** — Closed the two remaining "What you can build" sketches as real bundled topologies (dual-mode, committed CI record, tested) and wrote two forward-design docs. **tool_loop** — the classic model->tool->model agent loop as a topology (a ToolCall fires a tool Producer; its ToolResult re-fires the model with the result appended; ends on FinalAnswer). Best-in-class behaviors lifted from a READ of a real harness (opencode `tool.ts`/`max-steps.ts`, pulled from source via gh — not blog summaries): a failed tool surfaces as a typed `ToolResult(ok=False, error=...)` the model reacts to, never a crash (mirrors R-2's structured-error discipline), and a step budget fires a tools-disabled wrap-up so the run ALWAYS ends on a FinalAnswer, not a silent quiescence stop. Pure CI tools (a calculator computing (2+3)*4) -> committed, Level-2-replayable record; `walkthrough=True` swaps OllamaResponder. 4 tests (happy-path chain + result-fed-back, tool-error-as-observation, budget wrap-up, determinism). **game_of_life** — the simulation slot: Conway's Life as "many Producers acting each tick against a shared world-state Producer." A `world` Producer emits each Generation and fans it out as one CellTick per cell; a `cell` Producer per cell applies the Life rule against the shared grid (read via a KindBuffer View in the input_builder); a `step` Trigger (PerEvent + a per-tick count predicate) assembles the next grid once all cells report; bounded by a generation budget. CI: a 5x5 blinker over 2 generations (period-2 oscillator vertical->horizontal->vertical), 50 concurrent cell-Producers, committed record (264 frames, 53 decisions hash-verified). 2 tests. Both registered in `bundled.BUNDLED` (records auto-generate from the registry); both observation-verified (the record event sequences read correctly — see ## Sprint tail Rubber Duck Pass). **Docs:** `docs/tool-loop/tool-loop-futures.md` (parallel tool calls via a count predicate, human-in-the-loop approval via `pause_await_input`, sub-agents via `embedded_substrate`, durable agents, record-as-eval, error-recovery — each a small delta on shipped primitives, shipped-vs-sketch marked); `docs/design/substrate-as-a-tool.md` (Producers that author/run topologies — grounds `embedded_substrate` + the Studio's `build_from_spec` against the build-time freeze, proposes event-sourced topology amendment `substrate.TopologyAmended` as the open research direction). Also: the README "What you can build" accuracy pass (de-sketched the tool-loop + simulation bullets, dropped the `ast.parse` DIY-hedge — `coding_flow` is the real-gate sibling — and corrected the stale "first three ship" closer; all 8 bullets now ship), plus a CLI-backed-models research finding (the Responder seam takes ANY command-line model — Claude Code/Codex/Gemini via a CliResponder — verified against OpenDesign's `agent-adapters` as prior art; independence is per-request, "instances of a model" is a misframe). Dual contract: artifact — **314 passed + 1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict clean** on the new files; both topologies swept by the replay/pressure/bundled tests. App-level kinds (ToolCall/CellNext/...) are Producer-declared, not reserved `substrate.*` — no vocabulary-change-required. Pushed: `tool_loop` + the two agent/meta docs landed in commit `877ff54`; `game_of_life` + the README/topologies-README edits + the BLACKBOARD/KIT_DIARY record are working-tree (uncommitted at this writing).

- **coding_flow — best-of-N codegen + build-validation + correction loop (2026-06-19) — `src/substrate/topologies/coding_flow/`** — A NEW run-and-observe app rebuilding the prompt-factory precursor's pattern (construct → generate → parse-artifacts → BUILD-VALIDATE → grade → correct) as a Substrate topology, adding what concurrency makes free that the sequential precursor couldn't: N candidates drafted CONCURRENTLY from N DIFFERENT models (an ensemble), each build-validated in parallel against a free-form GATE, the passing one selected; a round with no pass feeds every gate failure back into a fresh round (bounded). Heterogeneous by the rule — only the DRAFTER is a model; seeder / validator / judge are deterministic code (truth is a test run, never the model's opinion of its own code). Five Producer kinds (seeder → N×Draft; drafter=MODEL → Candidate; validator=GATE-subprocess → Verdict; judge → Solved | N×Draft | Exhausted), 4 Triggers (draft + validate per-event; judge on round-complete), a `verdicts` KindBuffer View, terminate on `any_of(Solved, Exhausted)`. `gate.py` parses `# path:` artifacts (header-before-fence OR comment-inside-fence — a real-coder run EXHAUSTED until both were handled: the model put `# path:` INSIDE the fence and every candidate parsed to zero files), writes a sandbox temp dir, runs the gate (`ruff check && mypy --strict && pytest`), returns a NORMALIZED verdict (temp paths + timings stripped → a fixed candidate is reproducible despite the subprocess). NOT replay-deterministic (real subprocess timing → verdict order varies) → `deterministic=False`, NOT in BUNDLED (no committed record) — a run-and-observe demo, OUTCOME-deterministic in CI. 6 deterministic tests + a @realmodel ensemble walkthrough. **Verified live:** a [qwen3-coder:480b-cloud, qwen2.5-coder:7b, llama3:8b] ensemble → the two weak local models fail `mypy --strict`+`ruff`, qwen3-coder passes, the judge selects it by gate-pass (best-of-N over DIFFERENT models, winner chosen by build-validation, not a guess). Gate-green (ruff/format/mypy --strict/pytest); pushed, substrate matrix CI green on it.

- **Hardening + three reorgs + CI on both repos — the REVIEW.md application (2026-06-17→19) — `substrate/` + `substrate-ui/`** — The full list is the 2026-06-19 chapter close in ## Surfaced. Substrate-side: read-path data-loss detection (`RecordGapError` on a seq-gap / a torn sealed segment); the cancellable async Responder (kernel-correctness-1 — no orphaned worker thread / shutdown stall); `substrate validate` F-CLI-3 lints (undeclared-kind refs FAIL); the persistence cluster (seal-fsync via `_do_fsync`, fresh-Runtime-over-crashed-root recovery, `BlobStore.get` hash-verify); **product amendment A3 ×5** (resolve-in-spec — check-5 wall-clock half, F-LIFE-2 let-finish/subtree deferral, manifest fidelity, L1-replay, read-API); `src/substrate/` reorganized into `kernel/ record/ projections/ conformance/` subpackages (a resolver codemod rewrote ~80 imports; the import-linter denylist collapsed from 25 modules to the 4 subpackages — architecture-canon-2); `topologies/` co-located (each topology a code+records package; `conversation_demos.py` split); LICENSE (Apache-2.0) + `docs/README.md` index + README on-ramp. **CI:** substrate's existing matrix made to pass after a real `ruff format` drift, and the **`typing_extensions`** base-install bug fixed (python-ulid 3.x imports it undeclared — a base `pip install substrate` broke at import). All gate-green (ruff/format/mypy --strict/lint-imports/conformance 15-0-1-1); pushed to `laffeyp/Substrate`, matrix CI green. The UI-side of the same review (CSRF/XSS/Studio traps/trigger-slot wiring/readability) is recorded in `substrate-ui/process/BLACKBOARD.md`.

- **UI BUILD step 5 — RESUME (the control layer COMPLETE) (2026-06-15, Phase 2) — `substrate-ui/`** — Built the last control piece (ruling C1: launch + resume). New shared `substrate-ui/demo_topologies.py` mirrors the runtime's own pause/resume reference (`resumable_topology`: stage1 → PAUSE awaiting `ApprovalGranted` → a resume Trigger fires stage2 → quiescence finalise), used by both the generator (to CREATE the paused `demo_resumable` record, persistent) and the server (to RESUME it). `POST /api/resume?record=<name>` resumes a **COPY** (unique id) — reattaches to the copy's persistent bus, injects the external event so the resume Trigger fires the continuation, on the SAME seq sequence; backgrounded + tracked in `_LAUNCHES` so liveness/torn detection works exactly like launch. Resuming a copy keeps the paused TEMPLATE re-resumable. The records index carries `resumable` (= in `_RESUMABLE`); the console shows a header **"▶ resume"** button only for a paused+resumable run, which POSTs then loads/follows the resumed run. Verified: `demo_resumable` is `paused` (awaiting ApprovalGranted) → resume → a fresh `resume_<id>` run with `ApprovalGranted`+`Stage2Done`+`RunFinalised` on the bus → `finalised`; the template stays paused. Tested-live: `test_server.py` **18 pytest** (+ resume continues a paused run to finalised with the continuation events, template stays paused, + non-resumable→404), `e2e_console.js` (+ paused→resume button shown→click→FINALISED). ruff clean; substrate runtime untouched (281/conformance 15/0/1/1). Two honest observations flagged for review #38: (a) a resumed run shows stage1 as **"running"** in a finalised run (the pause interrupted its ProducerCompleted — a real pause artifact, started-not-completed; not a failure); (b) a transient "FINALISED · NOT CLEAN" verdict can flicker DURING resume (the console's separate run_graph/summary/events fetches briefly inconsistent) before settling to FINALISED — the check is lenient so it passes, but worth a look. **Review #38 = RESUME complete + honest (the ApprovalGranted + continuation land on the record's own dense seq sequence, template re-resumable, same run_id — verified); the ruled control set (launch + resume + live-attach) is DONE.** Folded its 3 findings: **(obs a, §7-honesty, fixed in the PROJECTION not the UI as the reviewer insisted)** a pause-interrupted Producer (started, no ProducerCompleted, run over) read "running" in a FINALISED run — a finished run showing live work; fixed `graph.py` to label an un-ended instance **"interrupted"** when the run is terminal/paused (vs "running" only in an INCOMPLETE run), added `.bar.interrupted` styling + a graph test + an e2e assertion (resumed run: 0 running lanes, stage1 interrupted); **(obs b, the transient NOT-CLEAN flicker — root-caused)** `loadRecords` ended with an UN-awaited `selectRecord(recs[0])` (= demo_broken, fails=1) that raced the explicit resume-select on shared STATE → a momentary false "NOT CLEAN"; fixed with a staleness guard in `selectRecord` (drop a stale result if a newer select started) + auto-select only on FIRST load (STATE.name===null), and an e2e that samples the verdict throughout resume asserting NO NOT-CLEAN flicker (flicker=false); **(Q1)** documented that resuming a COPY is a DEMO affordance (re-runnable fixture) — production resume is single-shot in-place. Deeper runtime question NOTED (not a UI blocker, my call): should the kernel emit a terminal for a Producer interrupted by a pause (stage1 is stranded started-without-end)? Substrate gate after the projection change: **282 passed, mypy 49, conformance 15/0/1/1**; server **18 pass**; e2e PASS (no flicker, interrupted rendered). **THE OBSERVE + CONTROL UI IS COMPLETE on the real runtime** (read records, watch live, provenance, diff, I/O, launch, resume — 8 review rounds #32–38, all folded). NEXT is the **Studio to FULL PARITY** (a visual TopologyBuilder: Producers/Triggers/Views/Predicates/Termination + Routes + `any_of`/`all_of` composition + REAL build&launch to a Runtime, per the E2 ruling) — needs a server-side topology-SPEC → TopologyBuilder → Runtime translator.

- **UI BUILD step 4 — backgrounded launch + LIVE-ATTACH (the signature live-watch surface) (2026-06-15, Phase 2) — `substrate-ui/`** — Built the live-watch piece (review #35 finding 3: synchronous launch couldn't deliver it). **Backgrounded the launch**: `POST /api/launch` spawns the run in a daemon thread (fresh loop), waits only until `RunStarted` is on the record, and returns IMMEDIATELY with the current `run_graph.status` (`incomplete` for a still-running run; for a fast bundled topology that already finished, `finalised`). Added a deliberately SLOW launchable topology (`live_demo` — a producer that `asyncio.sleep`s between 6 `LiveTick`s, ~3s) so live-attach has something to watch grow (caught a bug building it: the Struct was `_Tick` so the kind was `_Tick`, but the termination counted `"Tick"` → no match → `stuck_quiescent` — which incidentally proved a real run-level failure renders; renamed to `LiveTick`). **Live-follow** (`followLive` in app.js): after launching, the console polls the growing record (`run_graph`+events+summary) every 400ms, live-tails the cursor to the latest seq, and stops on the terminal — read-only over the same projections (attach/F-PERS-4 semantics). **Resolved the #33 INCOMPLETE residual** at exactly the right layer: a live-followed no-terminal run renders a pulsing **"● LIVE"** verdict (`v-live`), NOT amber-incomplete — the follow context IS the out-of-band signal that distinguishes incomplete-live (still being written) from incomplete-torn (broken); a STATIC incomplete record still reads amber. Tested-live: `test_server.py` **15 pytest** (+ backgrounded launch returns `incomplete` then the record grows to `finalised`), `e2e_console.js` (+ live launch → verdict `LIVE` while writing → `FINALISED` on terminal; fixed the fast-debate-launch assertion since fast runs return `finalised` not `incomplete`). ruff clean; substrate runtime untouched (281/conformance 15/0/1/1). **Review #36 = sound + honest on the happy path (race-free handshake, torn-safe polling, #35 unique-id fix confirmed, static-torn still amber) — but CONFIRMED the §7.2 hole I flagged: a run that TEARS mid-follow stayed "● LIVE" FOREVER (the LIVE signal was keyed on a follow-flag that never decays + a status that never leaves "incomplete" → a dead run reads as healthy). FOLDED both prescribed layers:** **(server-authoritative)** the server tracks each launch's thread (`_LAUNCHES`); `run_graph` now carries **`live`** (= the launch thread is alive) — a launch whose thread died with no terminal is `incomplete` + `live:false` = TORN; **(client staleness backstop)** `followLive` STOPS on a terminal, on `!live` (server says dead → "torn"), or on no-seq-growth for ~8s (alive-but-wedged → "stalled"); `renderVerdict` shows "● LIVE" ONLY for `incomplete && server-live`, so a torn run falls to amber "● INCOMPLETE" (broken). Built a real TORN fixture (`demo_torn` — a clean record with its terminal frame truncated) so the static-torn rendering is tested. Tested-live: `test_server.py` **16 pytest** (+ liveness true→false, + demo_torn incomplete+not-live), `e2e_console.js` (+ live→LIVE→FINALISED, + demo_torn→amber-INCOMPLETE-not-LIVE). Carried residual (the reviewer's finding 2, scoped to long-running live use, NOT now): live-attach polls the WHOLE record each 400ms (O(record²) over a long run) → switch to incremental `attach()`/`read_new()`/SSE before real long live watches. **Re-review #37 = torn hole CONFIRMED CLOSED** (server-authoritative `live` flag measured correct: torn→incomplete+live:false→amber, live run→LIVE→finalised+live:false; restart correctly marks a pre-restart in-flight run torn — in-memory `_LAUNCHES` is the CORRECT choice here, unlike the #35 counter). Clean to build RESUME. Folded its two follow-ups (cheap, latent footguns): **(staleness false-positive, my q3 — confirmed)** the 8s no-growth backstop would ABANDON a slow-but-alive LLM run (a long generation is `live:true` + no-growth for >8s — normal); fixed to NEVER abandon on no-growth — server-liveness (`live:false`) is the only authoritative stop; a not-growing-but-live run is annotated "● live · waiting (no growth Ns)" and kept followed (a wedged model call self-ends at the adapter timeout → a terminal); **(`_LAUNCHES` leak)** `_is_live` now evicts a dead thread on check (no unbounded growth). No regression: server **16 pass**, e2e PASS, ruff clean, substrate untouched (281/conformance 15/0/1/1). Carry-forward residuals (NOT now, before real-LLM/long-live use): incremental `attach()`/SSE instead of whole-record polling (O(record²) on a long run); a stubbed dynamic torn-mid-follow frontend test. The LAST control piece is **RESUME** (needs a resumable topology — a pause_await_input + a resume Trigger on the external event — and the persistent-bus `Runtime.resume` path; self-records like launch, targets a record by its unique id, backgrounded + tracked in `_LAUNCHES` so its liveness works the same) — the next increment.

- **UI BUILD step 3 — the thin control layer begins: LAUNCH (2026-06-15, Phase 2) — `substrate-ui/`** — The first surface that CAUSES something (per the launch+resume-only ruling C1; §7.7 control is thin + recorded). `server.py` gains `do_POST` + `POST /api/launch?topology=<name>` — it RUNS a bundled topology to a fresh `runs/launch_<name>_N.record` via `api.Runtime().run()` (a real run; the launch IS the recorded `RunStarted` at seq 0) and returns {name, status}; + `GET /api/topologies` (the launchable list). The console rail gains a `+ launch a topology… ▸ run` control that POSTs, then loads + selects the new run. Verified: launching from the UI creates a genuine code_review run (6 instances, status finalised) the console reads back. Tested-live-as-we-go: `test_server.py` now **13 pytest** (+ launch runs a real 6-instance record opening with RunStarted, + unknown-topology→404), `e2e_console.js` asserts the live launch (select debate → a new record appears, "launched → … · finalised"). ruff clean; substrate runtime untouched (281/conformance 15/0/1/1). **Review #35 = launch is HONEST (§7.7: a real run, RunStarted-recorded, real RunResult; nothing invented) and concurrency-safe (the reviewer stress-tested 5-way) — but caught ONE real bug: the in-memory launch counter + an rmtree-on-collision SILENTLY DELETED prior launched records on a server restart (counter resets → name collision → rmtree of a prior durable record = data loss on the artifact the whole product is about). FOLDED:** named launched records by a **unique id** (`launch_<name>_<uuid12>`) — no collision, so the counter, the lock, AND the rmtree-on-collision all DROP out (one move); removed the now-needless `.lock` cleanup (a non-persistent Runtime leaves none — verified the launched record reads clean). Pinned with a **durability test** (two launches → two distinct records, BOTH still readable — the data-loss bug staying fixed). Server **14 passed**; e2e launch shows the unique-id name; ruff clean. Reviewer's other notes deferred to the live-attach piece (background the run — synchronous can't deliver live-attach; do it WITH attach) and kept-as-is (launched records stay durable, NOT ephemeral — that's the product; the rail will want recency sort/filter at volume). NEXT: the live-control increment — background the launch (return immediately, status running/incomplete) + live-ATTACH/follow (the console follows the growing record via `attach()`/F-PERS-4 — exercises the INCOMPLETE branch / the #33 residual) + RESUME (self-records the same way, targets a record by its unique id). Then the Studio to full parity (per E2).

- **UI BUILD step 2 — the diff + I/O surfaces (2026-06-15, Phase 2) — `substrate-ui/`** — Built the two surfaces re-review #33 cleared, test-live-as-we-go. **DIFF** (`first_divergence`/D-8, semantics pinned #30 Q-E1): server `/api/diff?a=&b=` → divergence {index, seq, kind_a/b, hash_a/b} or D-8-equivalent; a header `⇄ diff vs…` selector renders it in the inspector (first divergence at seq N, or "equivalent under D-8"). Generated a real diverging pair (`demo_diff_a`/`_b` — two runs of one topology emitting [1,2,3] vs [1,2,9] → diverge at seq 5, different payload hashes). **I/O** (derived from the record, nothing invented — §7.1): server `/api/records/<name>/io` → input = the initial firing's `resolved_input` (the seed; honestly null for a build-parameterized topology), outputs = the application events as seq-cited artifacts, + the finalisation_payload; a header `I/O` toggle swaps the centre for an input-document + output-artifacts view (artifacts materialize as the seq-cursor reaches them). Generated `demo_solo_chat` (a real seed `{prompt:…}` in → one Message out) so the INPUT side is demonstrable. **Tests grew with the code (the standing directive):** `test_server.py` now **11 pytest** (+ diff: self→equivalent, a-vs-b→seq-5 divergence with differing hashes; + io: solo_chat seed→Message, code_review null-seed→4 cited artifacts), and `e2e_console.js` asserts both LIVE in the DOM (the diff seq-5 result + the I/O seed+Message). **Review #34 = both surfaces faithful + honest (diff a correct first_divergence — diverge seq 5, equivalent a-vs-a, cross-topology diverges at the RunStarted, 404 on a bad record; I/O invents nothing — real seed or null-shown-as-null, outputs seq-cited, finalisation from the payload), no regression, clear to productionize.** Folded its one finding: I/O omitted the **baseline** (`RunStarted.payload.baseline` — the substrate's designated b.baseline() seed/fixture channel); added it to `/io` + the input view (empty for the bundled records, but a baseline-seeded run would otherwise show null input), gave `demo_solo_chat` a real `baseline={dataset,seed}` to demonstrate+test it, and pinned the reviewer's two test gaps (the cross-topology diff diverging at the RunStarted; the io baseline). Final: **server 11 passed; live E2E PASS (15 records, §7 + diff + I/O + baseline checks, 0 console errors)**; substrate runtime untouched (281/mypy 49/conformance 15/0/1/1). **Before-arbitrary-topologies residual list (all small/scoped, none gate the current build): cohort cause-seq (group by parent+cause_seq), a real INCOMPLETE fixture (or live-attach).** NEXT (design progression: read→live→provenance/diff→light control→authoring): the **light-control + live-watch** layer — launch a bundled topology (records RunStarted) + resume a paused run + attach/follow (which exercises the INCOMPLETE branch), per the launch+resume-only ruling — then the Studio to full parity (Routes + `any_of`/`all_of`, per E2).

- **UI BUILD step 1 — the backend seam + a live console (2026-06-15, Phase 2) — `substrate-ui/`** — Architect said GO; built the production seam the whole UI rests on, end-to-end. **`substrate-ui/server.py`** — a read-API server (stdlib http.server + msgspec, **only `substrate.api`** — a UI reader on public surfaces, F-API-6 in spirit) that serves the REAL substrate read API as JSON: `/api/records` (the rail, each with the real run-level `run_graph.status`), `/api/records/<name>` (events + manifest), and `/run_graph` `/topology_graph` `/summary` `/narrate` `/explain/<producer>` (the tested projections + provenance). This REPLACES the prototype's embedded `data.js` + client-side `engine.js` reimplementation with the one tested Python source of truth — pointing it at live `attach()`/`runs/` is the same code, different record source. **`substrate-ui/web/`** (`index.html` + `app.js`, vanilla JS, no build step) — a live console that reads those projections and renders the core surfaces: the record rail (real status + §7.2 failure flags), the run-as-graph (firing-anchored lifespans off `fired_seq` + spawn-cohort bands "∥ N concurrent" — §7.3 concurrency-from-spawn, the projection's own guidance), the seq-cited colored event stream, the provenance inspector (`/explain` → cause + ancestry), and the health bar (verdict keyed on the real run-level `status`, so a failed/incomplete/paused run looks broken — §7.2). One seq-cursor drives the graph + stream in lock-step (scrub mid-run → partial run drawn, future events dimmed). **Verified by running in real Chrome (Playwright):** all 9 bundled records load over HTTP, the code_review cohort band + cancel-others story render off the live `run_graph`, provenance + scrub work, **0 console errors**. The de-risk projections (`run_graph`/`topology_graph` incl. `fired_seq` + the run-level `status` taxonomy) are now the live data contract — exactly as designed. Server on :8765. **Tested end-to-end LIVE (the standing directive): `substrate-ui/test_server.py` (9 pytest — real server on an ephemeral port, real `substrate.api` over HTTP, the served JSON pinned against the Python projections incl. the failure statuses) + `substrate-ui/e2e_console.js` (live Playwright E2E — real Chrome + real backend, §7 asserted in the DOM).** **Review #32 = faithful first increment; caught 4 real findings, ALL FOLDED + verified live:** **(1, headline §7.3)** the cohort heuristic grouped by `trigger_id` and under-detected natural_conversation's concurrency (one Turn spawns 4 producers via 4 different triggers but ONE parent → flattened to sequential); fixed to group by **shared PARENT** (+ consecutive `fired_seq`) — the reviewer verified parent bands every shape correctly; natural_conversation now reads "∥ 4 concurrent", code_review "∥ 5 concurrent". **(2, §7.2)** all bundled records are clean, so the red/cyan/amber failure branches were logic-only; added `gen_demo_records.py` producing THREE real non-clean records by actually running topologies (`demo_failed`=view_failure→`failed`, `demo_paused`→`paused`, `demo_broken`=producer-fails-inside→`finalised`+1 failure), a `runs/` scan in the server, and failure-status assertions in both test suites — §7.2 now demonstrable end-to-end (failed→red, paused→cyan over the wire). **(3)** the prominent verdict badge keyed only on run-level status → a finalised-with-failures-inside run read "FINALISED" (clean); fixed to "FINALISED · NOT CLEAN" when `producers_failed>0` (matches the engine.js it replaces + the health bar). **(4)** `server.py` claimed "substrate.api ONLY" but statically imports the bundled registry (demo-record enumeration, not the read seam) — scoped the docstring honestly. Final: **server tests 9 passed; live E2E PASS (12 records, the 4-way + 5-way cohort bands, failed/paused/NOT-CLEAN verdicts, provenance, lock-step scrub, 0 console errors)**. **Re-review #33 = all 4 folds CONFIRMED, no regression, clear to build I/O + diff.** TWO residuals SCOPED to the live/productionize seam (NOT blockers for the next surface, per the reviewer): **(a) cohort cause-seq** — the reviewer PROVED an over-merge: a parent emitting MULTIPLE triggering-event bursts of LEAF spawns (no intervening spawn) gets mis-banded "∥ N concurrent" by consecutive-same-parent though the bursts are sequential. No bundled topology hits this (each bundled parent fires exactly one burst — a fresh speaker/solver instance per round), so consecutive-same-parent ships correctly today; the robust general fix (before the seam reads ARBITRARY user topologies) is to group cohorts by **(parent, cause_seq)** where cause_seq = the triggering event's seq (the cause is appended just before the TriggerFired burst in the cycle — derivable from the record, likely no kernel change). **(b) INCOMPLETE fixture** — the amber no-terminal branch lacks a real test fixture (the 3 demos all have terminals); it's exercised naturally by live-attach (a mid-flight run has no RunFinalised → "incomplete"), or capture a deliberately-torn record. NEXT: build the **I/O surface** (input = resolved seed; outputs = application events + finalisation_payload as cited documents) + the **diff surface** (`first_divergence`/D-8, semantics pinned #30 Q-E1), test-live-as-we-go; carry the two residuals into the productionize/live-attach step; then Studio full parity (Routes + `any_of`/`all_of` + real launch, per E2).

- **UI design handoff — engineering acceptance + friction fixes (2026-06-15, Phase 2)** — The external design instance returned a strong, disciplined package (`handoff/`: V0 console frozen North Star + console-v2 with I/O surface + integrated terminal + solo_chat base case, the Topology Studio authoring surface, 3 styled docs, its own BLACKBOARD/KIT_DIARY following the same SDD method). **Engineering-accepted after fully RUNNING it** (real Chrome via Playwright over a local server — the prototypes need HTTP because Babel XHR-loads the `.jsx`): exercised every surface hands-on (all 5 runs, seq-cursor scrub mid-run, provenance click, SPLIT/GRAPH/STORY/I-O, the terminal, Studio wire+build+launch). §7 verified IN the running app — backed-by-seq (the event inspector shows seq+schema+raw payload), failures loud (broken run fully red incl. the terminal `narrate --summary`), concurrency honored (the "∥ 5 concurrent" spawn-cohort band with firing-anchored lifespans — the review #29 fix, realized), eight words + lens-not-controller. The data contract LANDED the de-risk dead-on: `engine.js` is a client-side reimplementation of our read API (deriveProducers≈run_graph, summarise≈narration_summary, explain≈explain_producer), the two graph surfaces map field-for-field to `topology_graph`/`run_graph`, and the paused run renders exactly the `status`/`paused_on` from the review #29 fold. **Friction fixes applied before review:** (design, V0 left frozen) fixed `studio/studio_core.js` generated code to match the real `TopologyBuilder` API (`producer_kind` was missing `schema_version` + `start=`/`factory=`; `trigger` was missing the required `input_builder=`); silenced the favicon 404 on console-v2 + studio. (runtime) **folded `fired_seq` into `run_graph.ProducerInstance`** — the `TriggerFired` seq (when scheduled), the t-free firing anchor the run-as-graph renders concurrency on; previously the design read it from raw events, now the projection carries it (`fired_seq <= started_seq`, test-pinned). **Resolved the Open Questions Register against the runtime** (in `handoff/ENGINEERING_ACCEPTANCE.md`): Q-A1 (Producer internals = application Python behind a Responder, only emitted events are visible — no kernel execution/tool mechanic), Q-B1 (attach = monotonic gap-free read-only follower, CRC-recovered tail, concurrent followers OK), Q-B2 (the 12-kind lifecycle set is CLOSED; the 4 failure kinds + run-level `RunFinalised{reason:view_failure}`; no kernel severities), Q-C1 (the runtime safely permits only launch + resume; cancel/inject/abort are not external controls), Q-E1 (`first_divergence` = D-8 log-equivalence, modulo run_id/instance/t) — RESOLVED; A2 (tool vocabulary), C2 (multi-user), E2 (Studio depth), and the targets for C1/D1 remain genuine PRODUCT calls. Net: no friction in the read/observe/control surfaces; the build is unblocked (seam = a thin read-API server `engine.js`/`data.js` call; then the diff surface). **Design package independently reviewed (#30): faithful, coherent, runs clean — A1/B1/C1/E1 resolutions verified accurate, data contract holds, vocabulary tight, no blocker mismarked.
