It caught TWO things I got wrong, both compounding on §7.2 — FOLDED:** **(finding 1, highest-stakes — my resolution was inaccurate)** Q-B2 named only `view_failure` as a run-level failure, but the runtime has THREE (`view_failure` / `kernel_error` / `stuck_quiescent`, all → `RunResult.status="failed"`, all emit `RunFinalised{reason}`); a UI keyed on the draft would render kernel-errored runs GREEN. Fixed `run_graph.status` to surface **"failed"** (distinct from a clean "finalised" that had Producer-level failures inside it — finished≠worked, which stays the per-instance statuses); corrected the Q-B2 resolution + fixed `console-v2/engine.js` verdict to flag run-level FAILED. **(finding 2 — my fix was incomplete)** the Studio termination codegen emitted descriptive STRINGS where typed args are required (`cancel_all_others(when="judge completes")`, `threshold_count(n="1")`) — they register but fail at runtime as `kernel_error` (exactly finding 1's missed case); fixed `studio_core.js` to emit typed args (numeric unquoted, `when=lambda ctx: ...`, `Subscription(kinds=frozenset({...}))`) + scoped the "runnable" claim (skeleton: shapes valid, author supplies bodies; `any_of` composition is the deferred Studio feature). Finding 2 gates only the deferred Studio launch, not the read build. Runtime gate after the folds: **281 passed/1 skipped; mypy --strict (49) + lint-imports KEPT + conformance 15/0/1/1 — green**. **Re-review #31 = both folds CONFIRMED CLOSED, no regression** — verified run_graph.status across all cases by running; found ONE precise residual (answering "did I miss any failure mode?"): a reasonless **4th failure mode** — the fsync-gate/torn-tail path fails a run by writing NO RunFinalised (absence-of-terminal IS the encoding of medium failure), which run_graph labelled "running" while the prototype engine labels "INCOMPLETE" — and production swaps engine→run_graph, so a torn record would regress from broken-looking to "running" (re-opening §7.2). FOLDED: `run_graph.status` no-terminal case is now **"incomplete"** (aligned with the engine), §7.2-safe — a static torn record never reads as a clean "running"; the still-writing-vs-torn distinction is inherently out-of-band (the live-follow context / the record layer's torn-tail signal), documented in the acceptance Q-B2. **Final run-level status taxonomy: {incomplete, paused, finalised, failed}** — the §7.2 "looks broken" guarantee now holds for ALL four failure modes (3 reasoned + 1 reasonless). Gate after this fold: **281 passed/1 skipped; mypy 49 + lint-imports KEPT + conformance 15/0/1/1 — green**. The design package is now genuinely frictionless for the read/observe/control build (both reviews' findings closed). NOT building yet — clear to start on the Architect's go + the product-call rulings (A2 tool vocab, C2 multi-user, E2 Studio depth).

- **Graph projections — the UI-build de-risk (2026-06-14, Phase 2) — `graph.py`** — Built the two read-side projections the handoff doc's §6 graph surfaces need but that didn't exist yet (the chapter-close de-risk, Architect-greenlit). Both are pure, deterministic, read-only projections (F-OBS-6) on the public surface, same pattern as `narrate` — accept a record root OR an iterable of envelopes, frozen `msgspec.Struct` results, cite seqs, no kernel import (F-API-6 KEPT). **`topology_graph(record) -> TopologyGraph`** — the STATIC structure from the RunStarted manifest: `ProducerNode`s (kind, the event kinds it emits, deterministic, `is_initial`), `TriggerEdge`s (`on` a subscription, firing policy, `starts` a Producer), `RouteEdge`s (id, slot), the View names, the TerminationPolicy descriptor. **`run_graph(record) -> RunGraph`** — the DYNAMIC run-as-graph from the lifecycle/provenance events: every `ProducerInstance` (kind, instance, parent, trigger_id, firing_key, input_sha256, started_seq..ended_seq SPAN, status completed/failed/cancelled/running, emitted app-event kinds), in spawn-seq order — the concurrent/causal/emergent shape (overlapping spans = concurrency; parent links = the spawn forest). 8 new public symbols on `substrate.api`; api.md regenerated (86 symbols, 15 groups). **Rubber Duck Pass across topology shapes CAUGHT a real correctness bug** before any review: `is_root` defined as "no Trigger starts this kind" reported ZERO entry points for a CYCLIC topology (natural_conversation's round-robin `after-N` trigger wraps to start speaker-1, so every kind is some trigger's target) — misleading the designer that the run has no entry point. Fixed: renamed to `is_initial`, sourced from the `TriggerFired{trigger_id=__initial__}` firings (the only record of which kinds are entry points — the manifest omits initials), which is correct for both acyclic (code_review: 5 reviewer entry points) and cyclic (natural_conversation: speaker-1) shapes; regression-pinned. Updated the handoff doc §8 "what exists to build on" to list both projections (the graph-view data is now ready-made, not just "in the record"). Dual contract: artifact — **12 graph tests + full suite 277 passed/1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict (49) + lint-imports (F-API-6 KEPT) + conformance 15/0/1/1 — green**. The UI's two graph surfaces now have their data contract whichever form the design lands on. **Review #29 = SHIP IT** (both projections correct/pure/well-cut across all SIX shapes the reviewer ran — is_initial holds on every cyclic topology incl. pair_coding/adversarial_pair back-edges; run_graph fully faithful spawn/span/status/emitted; static-vs-dynamic is the right split; nothing wrong on any shape). Folded its 3 findings: **(1, the one that matters — a flatten-the-concurrency trap)** run_graph's seq-spans faithfully encode bus-timeline concurrency, but in the DETERMINISTIC CI fixtures the single writer serializes near-instant Producers so span-overlap is sparse (code_review's 3 fast reviewers have DISJOINT spans) — a UI builder rendering concurrency from span-overlap alone, testing against those fixtures, would draw concurrent Producers as a sequence (the handoff §7.3 trap). The correct signal is the SPAWN STRUCTURE (siblings of one firing / adjacent spawn seqs — the 5 reviewers all share trigger_id=__initial__). Added that guidance to `ProducerInstance`'s docstring + handoff §8 (and CORRECTED the §8 bullet I'd written, which had said "the overlapping spans are the concurrency" — the trap itself). **(2)** typed the no-manifest error: `topology_graph` now raises `RecordIncompleteError` (a `SubstrateError` subtype) not a bare `ValueError`, so an api-only consumer catches it by type like the rest of the read surface. **(3)** surfaced run-level outcome on `RunGraph`: replaced `finalised: bool` with `status` ("running" | "paused" | "finalised") + `paused_on` (the resume_condition) — so the handoff's outcome surface can distinguish paused-awaiting-input from still-running from one projection, reading the `TerminationMatched{pause-await-input}` beat. Final artifact: **13 graph tests + full suite 278 passed/1 skipped; mypy --strict (49) + lint-imports KEPT + conformance 15/0/1/1 — green**.

- **UI design handoff document (2026-06-14, Phase 2) — `docs/design/ui-design-handoff.md`** — A self-contained brief for an external cloud Claude design instance (which will produce the UI, possibly with separately-supplied images for visual direction) that has ZERO Substrate context and won't grok the internals. Per the Architect's direction: **full vision in the doc** (observe + record-reader + light run control as the solid core, authoring-in-the-UI as the ambitious horizon) with **scope-phasing explicitly flagged as the owner's call, not decided in the doc**; **form-open** (substance precise, visual medium left to the designer + images). Contents: the one-paragraph mental model; the five conceptual leaps a designer must internalize (no central plan / emergent-reactive; one ordered timeline of concurrent actors; the product is an artifact not a session; everything typed + failures-are-events; deterministic/replayable); the 8-word vocabulary as the nouns to render (with the anti-synonym contract — no agent/workflow/step/node); a real code_review narration as the legibility anchor; the 3 personas (Observer/Debugger/Author) + 9 jobs; the probable form-agnostic surfaces (live stream, story view, structure graph, run-as-graph/timeline, provenance, outcome/health, diff, controls, authoring); the 7 non-negotiable constraints that back every pixel (read-the-record-never-the-runtime's-mind + seq citations; nothing-silent incl. finished≠worked; honor concurrency; the 8 words; no anthropomorphism; scale to volume; lens-not-controller); what already exists to consume (read_record / live attach / narrate+summary / explain+ancestry / view_at / diff / replay); a full end-to-end worked example (code_review as structure AND as run); and appendices cataloguing the lifecycle event kinds + the reader surface. Written to the project writing standard (plain register, de-LLM'd, define-before-use, scope-correct). Fact-checked against the live system (bundled topology list + CLI command surface verified). **Review #28 (fidelity + clarity) = SHIP IT** — the reviewer verified the load-bearing claims against the codebase (Appendix A is the exact 12-kind catalogue; every CLI command real; the 8-word definitions, the code_review example seqs, and the 5 leaps/7 constraints all accurate) AND confirmed the doc, read cold, "actively ARMS the designer against every wrong-design trap" (left-to-right pipeline, controller-not-lens, hidden failures, flattened concurrency, renamed vocabulary — each named and forbidden). Folded its findings: **(top fix, C2)** added live-event PACE to the signature live surface — events arrive at HUMAN speed (LLM-latency-bound, a few/sec with short bursts), design for watchability not a firehose; this was the one silence that could have made a designer over/under-build the hardest surface; **(C1)** noted Views/Predicates are internal — rendered as their EFFECT (a condition held → a Trigger fired), not as their own scenes; **(F1)** restored the §4 example's dropped `seq 24 finalise-run` beat + clarified §9 + flagged the block as payload-trimmed-but-seqs-exact, so a designer who runs `narrate` finds it matches to the letter; opened §6 leaning on §5's jobs (the one overlap the reviewer felt). This is the precursor artifact for Waves 10/12 (TUI/UI) — the UI build stays PAUSED pending the designer's output + the Architect's images.

- **API-sugar batch — review #23 items (2)–(4), Architect-ratified + implemented (2026-06-14, Phase 2)** — The Architect ratified the three remaining additive (non-breaking) authoring-ergonomics recommendations from review #23. **(2) `producer_kind(start=fn)` sugar + public `ProducerFactory`** — `producer_kind` now takes EITHER `start=` (the Producer callable itself, for the stateless case — sugar for `factory=lambda: fn`) OR `factory=` (the zero-arg callable, for the stateful/configured case); exactly-one enforced at build (`RegistrationError`). New public type alias `substrate.api.ProducerFactory = Callable[[], Producer]` so authors stop re-declaring `_Factory = Callable[[], Any]` and a wrong `factory=fn` (vs `lambda: fn`) is a type error. **Provenance bonus:** the `start=` wrapper copies the Producer's `__qualname__`/`__name__`, so the RunStarted manifest names the ACTUAL producer (e.g. `_ticker`) — strictly better than `factory=lambda`'s `<lambda>`. **(3) `b.instrument(name, *, on, schemas, input_builder, factory|start, into?, via?, stages?)` builder helper** — collapses the identical producer_kind + trigger + (optional) route triple the emergence instruments repeat into ONE call: a side Producer started once per `on` event (predicate-true, PerEvent), with an optional Route staging its emitted event into `into` (kind defaults to the sole schema's name; `via` defaults to the payload). Omit `into=` for an observation-only instrument (e.g. the grader). Migrated conversation.py's 3 instruments (common-ground/repair/grader) to it — behaviour-equivalent (same producers/events/order/payloads; only the trigger ids became the cleaner derived names, which read BETTER: trigger id == what it starts); regenerated the 9 bundled records from the single source. For a non-trivial predicate/policy, the explicit producer_kind+trigger+route remains. **(4) `Responder` promoted to `substrate.api`** — relocated the `Responder` Protocol from `reference/_models.py` to `protocols.py` (its natural home — a user-implemented structural protocol like Producer/View, though an application seam not a kernel primitive), re-exported from `reference` for back-compat. This AVOIDS the api→reference layering cycle (api imports protocols, which imports only types) the earlier reference/ re-export sidestepped — now the model seam is on the kernel's public surface cleanly. **F-API-6 KEPT** (the CLI/api surface stays acyclic). 2 new public symbols (`ProducerFactory`, `Responder`); api.md regenerated (78 symbols, 14 groups); `gen_api_docs.py` grouping extended. Dual contract: artifact — **9 new api-sugar tests + full suite 265 passed/1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict (48) + lint-imports (F-API-6 KEPT) + conformance 15/0/1/1 — green**. This closes ALL of review #23 (item 1 ctx shipped earlier; 2–4 now done). **Review #27 = SHIP IT** (all four claims held under falsification — start= names the real fn + exactly-one-of enforced; instrument() wires exactly the triple; migration `first_divergence` None with the committed record regenerated to match the relabelled trigger ids — which ARE D-8 decision identity under A1.2, so None proves regeneration not id-ignoring; Responder relocated, same-object back-compat, no cycle). Folded its one substantive nit: **instrument()'s stage Route was scoped by the emitted KIND, so a second producer emitting the same kind would cross-stage** (reviewer reproduced InjectionApplied=2). Because subscription matching is OR over kinds/producers (`sequencer.subscribed`), the fix is to scope the Route to the instrument's OWN producer — `Subscription(producers={name})` — which stages only what THAT instrument emits (its producer emits only its declared schemas); dropped the now-incompatible `stages=` kind-filter (selective multi-kind staging falls to the explicit route, documented). Behaviour-preserving for the single-emitter instruments (natural_conversation still `first_divergence` None); regression-pinned with a cross-emitter test (an instrument + a separate same-kind producer → only the instrument's emissions staged). Final artifact: **265 passed/1 skipped; mypy --strict (48) + lint-imports KEPT + conformance 15/0/1/1 — green**.

- **Narration — the legible prose projection (Wave 14, 2026-06-14, Phase 2)** — `substrate narrate <record>` + `substrate narrate --summary`, backed by a new pure read-only projection `substrate.api.narrate(record, *, lifecycle=False) -> Iterator[NarrationLine]` and `narration_summary(record) -> NarrationSummary` (new module `narrate.py`, mirroring the `inspect.py` idiom exactly: `_load` accepts a root path OR an iterable of envelopes, frozen `msgspec.Struct` results, F-OBS-6 read-only — no bus emission, no record mutation). **What it is:** the data layer a UI renders and the engine behind the CLI command — turns a log into the STORY of the run. Renders the substrate.* causal beats in prose (TriggerFired → "starts X", TerminationMatched, RunFinalised, and EVERY authoring failure — ProducerFailed/InputBuildFailed/PredicateQuarantined/ProducerEmittedInvalidEvent) interleaved with the application events (the work: "reviewer-security -> CritiquePosted (role=security, severity=4)"). **Suppresses the lifecycle bracketing** (ProducerStarted/Completed/InjectionApplied) by default — the trigger beat already says a producer started and the work event says what it did; `--lifecycle` restores the every-frame account (a strict superset, test-pinned). `--summary` emits a one-glance digest (finalised? + producer counts + failure tally + application-event counts) — a finalised-but-broken run reads as broken (the failure tally), parity with `run`'s WARNING surfacing. **Determinism:** the wall-clock `t` and supplementary metadata never enter the prose (test-pinned: no `t=`/`'t':` leak); ASCII output register, no emoji, every line cites a seq (CLI discipline). **Renderer fidelity:** beat renderers built against the REAL kernel payload shapes (read out of `sequencer.py`/`runtime.py` emit sites), and proven end-to-end — narrating the committed code_review record reads the cancel-others plot legibly (5 reviewers start → 3 critiques → judge blocks → 2 slow reviewers cancelled → finalise), and a live failing topology renders "boomer FAILED: RuntimeError('deliberate')" + a finalised/failed=1 summary. **F-API-6 preserved** — the CLI reaches narration only via `api.narrate`/`api.narration_summary` (lint-imports KEPT). 4 new public symbols (`narrate`, `narration_summary`, `NarrationLine`, `NarrationSummary`); api.md regenerated (76 symbols, 14 groups); `gen_api_docs.py` grouping extended. Dual contract: artifact — **19 narration tests + full suite 251 passed/1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict (48 files) + lint-imports (F-API-6 KEPT) + `substrate conformance --no-perf` 15/0/1/1 — green**. **Review #26 = EARNS ITS PLACE** (all four design claims held under falsification — pure projection, fidelity incl. forward-compat unknown-kind rendering, correct lifecycle cut, F-API-6 kept; degrades gracefully on every adversarial edge; the cancel-others story lands COLD; reviewer built a 3-way-failing topology and confirmed every failure beat reads correctly). Folded its 3 findings: **(1, highest-leverage — closes the #19 "finalised != worked" thread at last)** the `--summary` HEADER now shows the failure count on LINE 1 ("Run finalised WITH 3 FAILURES: N events") instead of reading green and burying the alarm on line 3 — a finalised-but-broken run now LOOKS broken at a glance; **(2)** the pause beat renders the typed `resume_condition` ("Run paused -- awaiting: <condition>.") instead of a bare "pause-await-input" — the load-bearing fact about a pause is WHAT it awaits; **(3)** default-mode `narrate` now footers a `[warning] N authoring failure(s) above -- run ... --summary` when failure beats scroll past in a long run. Self-caught BEFORE the review (Rubber Duck Pass across all 6 topology shapes): a free-text payload newline (`CodeChunk.text`) broke the one-beat-per-line contract — fixed `_short` to escape `\n`/`\r`/`\t`, regression-pinned. Reviewer's verdict on cuts: "CUT: nothing — the surface is minimal and lean. Don't grow it." Final artifact: **256 passed/1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict (48) + lint-imports KEPT + conformance 15/0/1/1 — green**. NARRATION LEG OF WAVE 14 COMPLETE. The interactive tutorial (the other leg) remains; the TUI stays PAUSED for the user's UI design spec.

- **`ctx` authoring-API revision — trigger callbacks unified behind TriggerContext (2026-06-14, Phase 2; Architect-ratified breaking change from review #23)** — The Architect ratified review #23's top recommendation ("delightful is what we do"). Replaced the asymmetric trigger callbacks `predicate(event, views)` + `input_builder(views, staged, event)` with a single typed `TriggerContext`: `predicate(ctx) -> bool` / `input_builder(ctx) -> input`, exposing `ctx.event` / `ctx.views` / `ctx.staged`. **New public type** `substrate.api.TriggerContext` (a frozen dataclass in `protocols.py`). **Kernel:** ONE `TriggerContext` constructed per appended event in `sequencer._eval_triggers`, shared by every Trigger's predicate + input_builder (allocates once per event, not per eval — the hot-path concern dissolves; predicates also gain harmless read access to `staged`). Two call sites changed. **Migration:** a libcst codemod (formatting-preserving, multi-line-safe — a regex would have wrongly hit route transforms, which KEEP `lambda event:`) migrated 84 lambdas across 19 files; + 3 named functions (r2's `_to_transform_input`/`_retry_input`/`_escalate_input`, renaming a colliding local `ctx`→`failure`); + 3 named predicates (`slow_pred`/`_slow_predicate`/`_raise` in conformance + test_inspect); + the conversation loop's default-arg closures extracted to mypy-friendly factories; + 2 lambdas inside test_cli's string-literal module. **Routes UNCHANGED** (`transform=lambda event:` — single-arg, no asymmetry). **Docs:** the tutorial's "two callback shapes to get right" warning is DELETED, replaced by one uniform `ctx` (the delight made visible); `api.md` now renders `Callable[[TriggerContext], bool]` + the TriggerContext docstring instead of the opaque `Callable[..., bool]` (the legibility win review #23 wanted). api.md regenerated (72 symbols). **Behavior unchanged** — pure calling-convention refactor: determinism holds (all `first_divergence` tests pass), committed records still replay. Dual contract: artifact — **232 passed + 1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict (47 files) + lint-imports (F-API-6 KEPT) + `substrate conformance --no-perf` 15/0/1/1 — green, the acceptance gate holds**. **Re-review #25 = SHIP IT** (correct + complete + behavior-preserving — the reviewer proved 5/5 fresh-vs-committed records D-8-identical, no old-signature callback left, api.md/tutorial correctly simplified). Folded its 2 follow-ups: **(B, migration-attributable)** wrapped `ctx.views`/`ctx.staged` in `MappingProxyType` so the public frozen `TriggerContext` is truly read-only — a predicate (which now reaches `staged`) cannot mutate the live run state, and the runtime now matches the documented `Mapping` field type (`sequencer.py`); **(A, pre-existing, surfaced by the check)** single-sourced `gen_topology_records.py` off the bundled registry + gave `bundled._code_review()` the demo `slow_roles`+`linger`, so `demo run` reproduces `demo replay` for all 9 bundled topologies (code_review now fresh==committed, cancel-others demonstrated LIVE, not just in the canned record). Remaining #23 items (2) `start=` sugar, (3) `b.instrument()` helper, (4) Responder→api: still open (additive, smaller).

- **Performance review #21 (dedicated DIMENSION) + fold (2026-06-14, Phase 2)** — Dedicated perf/scalability audit, MEASURED. - **Security review #20 (dedicated DIMENSION) + fold — HIGH path-traversal vuln fixed (2026-06-14, Phase 2)** — First dedicated security audit. Found one REAL **HIGH-severity** vuln (the prime suspect): a crafted/corrupt RECORD's `$blob` field path-traverses to **ARBITRARY FILE READ** via `substrate replay --level 2` — `replay.py` re-derived a filesystem path from the record's `$blob` hex with NO validation (and `BlobStore._path_for` had the same latent bug); CRC is integrity-not-authenticity, so a malicious record passes `read_record` intact. Reviewer confirmed with a PoC (read a file outside the record root). This broke the core trust boundary — a record is meant to be inert, shareable DATA. FIXED: `is_blob_hex()` validates the digest as `^[0-9a-f]{64}$` at the choke point (`blobstore._path_for`, covering get/put) AND in replay's resolver (→ `_UNRESOLVABLE` on invalid), + a within-root `is_relative_to` backstop. Test: `test_security_blob_traversal.py` (3, incl. the exact PoC). MED folds: `score` catches a malformed/crafted Grade payload → clean `[config]` error (was an uncaught `float()` traceback); `run`/`resume --topology-module --help` now states the code-exec posture. Reviewer CLEARED (tried, held): reserved-namespace spoof (triple-enforced — a Producer cannot forge a `substrate.*` event), corrupt-frame drop-safe, NO record→code-exec path, no SSRF. Dual contract: artifact — **232 passed + 1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict (47) + lint-imports (F-API-6 KEPT) + conformance 15/0/1/1 — green**. NEXT dimension: performance/scalability.

Built, all on public `substrate.api`: **`conversation.py`** — the general N-speaker turn-based substrate (round-robin `Turn` cascade via one Trigger per speaker, transcript View, `Converged` early-stop via threshold_count, round budget) with optional emergence INSTRUMENTS (common-ground + repair side Producers fired per Turn and Routed back into the next speaker — the emergence wiring). **`instruments/scoring.py`** — proper scoring rules (Brier/log-loss/spherical), properness numerically tested (the property that closes the cheap-talk loop). **`instruments/common_ground.py` + `repair.py`** — typed-event side Producers (non-load-bearing). **`conversation_demos.py`** — debate (positional asymmetry), prisoners_dilemma (payoff), intel_asymmetry (information), natural_conversation (the WITH/WITHOUT instrument ABLATION — the flagship composition demo; the delta in event kinds + turn text IS the demonstration). **`bundled.py` + cli hook** — `substrate run --topology <name>` now works for all 7 bundled topologies (review #16 HAT B: was an empty registry / dead end); the CLI imports `bundled` DYNAMICALLY so F-API-6 (cli → api only) stays KEPT; `topologies/README.md` adds discoverability (run + record paths). **Review #16 HAT A folded:** corrected the F1 shared-wrong-model (see ## Surfaced) — `code_review` termination comment + the liveness-test rationale now state the true semantics (all_completed finalises failures via ended_total; watchdog is a defensive zero-Producer backstop; the only real residual is a wedged-running-forever Producer, bounded by the model timeout). - **Wave 11 pressure + E2E hardening + F1 watchdog correction (2026-06-13, Phase 2)** — `tests/test_topology_pressure.py` (18 tests, full suite now 182): **E2E** via `CliRunner` over the committed records (`substrate replay --level 2`, `tail` — real exit codes); **determinism** (`first_divergence` is None across two independent runs of all three topologies — D-8 log-equivalence holds under concurrency); **liveness** (the real F1); boundary quorums (1, N), single/many-chunk pair_coding, depth-1 + high-fanout (4×3 = 84-node) recursion, runaway-at-shallowest-depth, a failing-model reviewer. **Pressure testing corrected a wrong mental model (mine AND review #15's phrasing):** I'd added `quiescence_with_watchdog` for F1 believing the watchdog was a hard deadline; `policies.py:121` shows it finalises only on `quiescent and running==0`, and `seconds` is the idle-poll window. So the watchdog DOES fix the FAILURE path (a `ProducerFailed` is an end that is NOT a completion, so `all_completed` — needing `completed>=started` — can never fire and would HANG; `quiescence_with_watchdog` finalises on `running==0` regardless of how each Producer ended), but a Producer stuck RUNNING forever keeps `running!=0` and is NOT force-finalised by v1.0 — liveness there rests on the model adapter timeout (`OllamaResponder` 120s). The code comment + the liveness test were corrected to state this honestly (the first version wrongly simulated stuck-forever and hung the test — the pressure test caught it). `code_review` watchdog window is now a param. Artifact: **full suite 182 passed + 1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict (38 files) + lint-imports + `substrate conformance --no-perf` 15/0/1/1 — all green/unchanged**. Review #16 dispatched (code review + a real-user acceptance pass — the reviewer now also uses substrate cold, as a newcomer, and reports the first-run UX). Closed clean.

- **S-11.INT — Wave 11 close + review #15 fold (2026-06-13, Phase 2)** — Committed CI-mode records for all three topologies under `src/substrate/topologies/<name>/records/ci_mode.record/` (`scripts/gen_topology_records.py`, deterministic, no network) + a Level-2 replay test per topology (`tests/test_topology_replay.py`: fresh run → `assert_replayable(record, "2")`, every recorded `TriggerFired` resolved-input re-hashes to its `input_sha256`, D-5; `replay --level 3b` asserted to raise NotImplementedError — never faked). `substrate replay <committed record> --level 2` verified by hand: 26 frames, 6 decisions verified. **Independent review #15 = PASS** (reviewer ran its own `Runtime→read_record` harness on all three and confirmed the cancellation/injection/budget facets genuinely fire on the records, the vocabulary call is right, both deviations honestly surfaced). **Folded review #15's one finding (F1, MID — a liveness gap I missed):** `code_review` had dropped the `quiescence_with_watchdog` backstop its card specifies, so an unreachable quorum (enough reviewers failing before they emit → live count < quorum → judge never fires) could hang the run; restored `any_of(cancel_all_others, all_completed, quiescence_with_watchdog(seconds=60))` (a TerminationPolicy timer, NOT a Trigger WallClock cooldown — verified the record's `replay_ceiling` stays `3a`). **Reconciled `PHASE2_PLAN.md` to the de-landmined cards (review #14 carryover C1):** S-11.INT/S-13.INT `Level 3(b)`/`byte-identical` → Level 2 (3(b) noted as the deferred A1.1 non-gate); `rostrum *` command surface → `substrate *` (the shipped entrypoint; resolves the unratified-rename trap by reverting to the real CLI, B-Q-1 still open). Dual contract: artifact — **full suite 164 passed + 1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict (38 files) + lint-imports (F-API-6 KEPT) green; `substrate conformance --no-perf` = 15/0/1/1 unchanged**. Wave 11 closes clean; the only open items are the two surfaced dispositions awaiting Architect ratification (pair_coding gate deviation; DepthBudgetExceeded as an application kind). - **Wave 11 — priority topologies (code_review, pair_coding, recursive_decomposition), CI mode (2026-06-13, Phase 2)** — The three user-named topologies, built ONLY on public `substrate.api` (the same surface a third-party author has — F-API-6 dogfooding), each an r1/r2/r3-pattern factory using the `DeterministicResponder` (CI) / `OllamaResponder` (walkthrough) seam. Files: `src/substrate/topologies/{__init__,code_review,pair_coding,recursive_decomposition}.py` + `tests/test_{code_review,pair_coding,recursive_decomposition}_topology.py`. **code_review** = R-1 ensemble with role-distinct reviewers (security/performance/style/correctness/clarity) → a Bus-view quorum predicate fires a judge (Once) emitting `VerdictRendered` → cancel-all-others on the judge's completion (demonstrated on its OWN record via lingering reviewers — 2 real `ProducerCancelled`) → all_completed. **pair_coding** = driver streams `CodeChunk`+`ChunkBoundary` in chunked instantiations; navigator (fired per `CodeChunk`, never its own `Suggestion` — no self-feedback) reviews the accumulating-code View and emits `Suggestion`; a Route stages it (`substrate.InjectionApplied`) into the driver's NEXT instantiation's recorded resolved input. **recursive_decomposition** = planner → ONE PerEvent Trigger spawns solvers at any depth (recursive-Trigger property, D-17); a complementary depth-budget Trigger fires a guard emitting `DepthBudgetExceeded` for any subtask beyond `max_depth`, bounding even a runaway solver ON THE LOG. Sprint cards 130/131/132 de-landmined first: the `replay --level 3b` acceptance lines → `--level 2` (3(b) byte-identity is the deferred A1.1 item, NOT a gate — `replay(level="3b")` raises NotImplementedError and conformance check 6 FAILs if it does not defer), and `rostrum tail` → `substrate tail` (the shipped entrypoint; `rostrum` is nowhere in src — B-Q-1 unresolved). Topology event kinds are Producer-declared + strict-validated at the bus boundary (same convention as R-1/R-2/R-3's `Candidate`/`Verdict`/`Row`/`CodeChunk`) — NOT a `process/signals/0.2.json` change. Dual contract: signal — each topology's record asserted structurally (quorum-then-verdict + 2 cancellations; chunk count + suggestion-reaches-next-chunk via InjectionApplied; one-trigger-all-depths + depth distribution + runaway-bounded); artifact — **10 new tests green; full suite 161 passed + 1 skipped; ruff + mypy --strict (topology pkg) + lint-imports (F-API-6 KEPT) green; `substrate conformance --no-perf` = 15 PASS / 0 FAIL / 1 DEFERRED (check 6, A1.1) / 1 SKIPPED — UNCHANGED**. - **Persistent pause/resume (F-TERM-3 / F-PERS-2) + review-#7 demo rebuilds (2026-06-13, Architect ruling — the agreed last v1.0 feature)** — The resume re-entry that was the one base-spec MUST gap. **Library:** `Runtime.resume(topology, *, resume_event)` (`runtime.py`) — refactored `run()` into `run()`/`resume()`/`_drive()`; resume requires `persistent=True` (config-time `RegistrationError`), re-acquires the flock, recovers the torn tail (`recover_open_segment`), reopens the EXISTING hot segment for append (`RecordWriter(resume=True)` — continues the same segment/seq, rebuilds the sealed list; `record.py` `_segment_index`), folds the registered Views Level-1 over the record + restores `next_seq` from the log tail + restores the original `run_id` + the started/ended counts (`_resume_bootstrap`), then injects the external resume event as a producer=null bus event (reserved-kind refused) so the resume Trigger fires the continuation → drives to terminal. Seq continues unbroken across the pause boundary; the manifest updates. **CLI:** `substrate resume <root> --topology-module M:func --input M:event_factory` (`cli.py`) replaces the exit-64 stub (shared `_load_attr` + `_drive_maybe_tailing`; exit 0/1/2/64/65/130). **`any_of` fix:** now propagates a composed member's `resume_condition` + tightest `watchdog_seconds` (`policies.py`) — a pause decided through the wrapper records its typed resume_condition. **N-MEM-1 documented:** `PerKey._seen` grows O(distinct keys) unbounded for the lifetime of a run (the cost of fire-once-per-key-forever); not bounded in v1.0 because LRU/window eviction silently lets an evicted key re-fire (a dedup-correctness change needing a decision) — operational guidance + the `staged`-is-bounded clarification in the `PerKey` docstring (`triggers.py`). **F-SCHEMA-2 EXERCISED** (`test_resume.py`): two runs at schema_version 1 and 2 under one persistent-bus root, each decoded via its OWN RunStarted manifest (`E@1`/`E@2`), no cross-run reinterpretation — honest about the one-run-per-record-root granularity. **Review #7 (coordinator-relayed, accepted as engineering guidance — NOT user authority): the reference DEMOS now demonstrate the mechanisms they name.** **R-2 rebuilt** (`reference/r2_pipeline.py`) as the real integrated cascade on a PERSISTENT bus — a REAL fault (undeclared-kind emission → `ProducerEmittedInvalidEvent`, no fabricated event reaches the bus); retry enriched via a Route carrying the failure reason (check-1 pattern) → recovers on attempt 2; an unrecoverable fault exhausts a per-row retry budget (`_InvalidByRow` View) → `RetryExhausted`; a malformed row's input_builder raises → `InputBuildFailed`; `RetryExhausted` → `pause_await_input` → `Runtime.resume` continuation. **R-1 fixed** (`reference/r1_ensemble.py`, by sub-agent) — lingering members (`slow_members`/`linger_seconds`) so cancel-all-others fires on R-1's OWN log; test renamed `test_r1_cancels_lingering_losers_on_adjudication` asserts 2 real `ProducerCancelled`. **Walkthroughs RE-RUN for real against Ollama + documented** (`docs/walkthroughs/README.md`): R-1 — weak models genuinely DISAGREE (Charisma/Vision/Integrity, 3 of 3) + 2 live cancellations; R-2 — full cascade + pause→resume end to end (continuous seq across the boundary). Tests: pause→resume in a FRESH Runtime + continuous-seq + run_id-preserved + reserved-kind-refused + CLI two-invocation resume (`test_resume.py`, `test_cli.py`). Dual contract: artifact — **148 tests + 2 perf, four gates green, `substrate conformance` = 16 PASS / 0 FAIL / 1 DEFERRED / 0 SKIPPED (perf 56,291 ≥ 40K), mypy --strict clean (34 files)**. F-TERM-3/F-PERS-2 CLEARED. Closed clean.

- **N-PERF-1 re-baseline (A2) + crc-splice fail-loud guard (2026-06-13, Architect ruling + review #6)** — Architect re-baselined N-PERF-1 (the 100K floor was derived from a prototype that never measured the required canonical encoding): cut `docs/specs/product_spec/draft7_amendment_A2_nperf1.md` — **floor 100K → 40K appends/sec** (~28% margin under the measured ~56K), recorded that the per-append cost is the pure-Python rfc8785 encoder and a compiled JCS encoder is the post-1.0 lever (rfc8785 LEFT AS-IS — byte-identity contract). `conformance.py` check-15 floor → 40K (PASSES honestly at ~56K); `test_perf.py` floor → 40K + xfail REMOVED (it passes); `process/WORKING_AGREEMENT.md` perf line + amendments list updated. **Review #6 fold (byte-identity guard):** `framing.py` `frame()` now FAILS LOUD if any top-level envelope key sorts before `"crc"` (the crc-splice is correct only because `crc` sorts first; a mis-spliced frame still verifies, so this guard is the only fail-loud catch for a future schema change that would silently break canonical bytes); `test_framing.py` adds the guard test + unicode/JCS-escape payload cases (non-ASCII, embedded quote/backslash/control, U+2028/U+2029) nailing payload-independence. `subtree_cancellation` confirmed a distinct unbuilt F-LIFE-2 recipe (NOT conflated with cancel_all_others) → ## Deferred. Dual contract: artifact — **all four gates green; `substrate conformance` (with perf) = 16 PASS / 0 FAIL / 1 DEFERRED (check 6) / 0 SKIPPED**. Closed clean.

*Agent appends one entry per sprint close. Append-only.*

- **N-PERF-1 optimization: batch-drain + crc-splice (2026-06-13, Architect "optimize first" ruling)** — Before/after at the faithful reference shape (50 Triggers across 10 kinds, ≤5 substantive/append): **~37K → batch-drain ~37K (no gain) → crc-splice ~56K appends/sec**; still under the 100K floor → HALTED + surfaced for the Architect's re-baseline call (see ## Surfaced for review). Files: `runtime.py` (writer-loop BATCH DRAIN — one `await inbox.get()` then a `get_nowait()` loop processes the ready batch before re-awaiting; total order / per-emission credit release / step-6 / quiescence all preserved; releases credits for unprocessed emissions on a mid-batch finalise so no task hangs on the cancellation gather), `framing.py` (CRC-SPLICE — `B_disk` = `B_hash` with `"crc":"<8hex>",` spliced after the opening brace since JCS sorts `crc` first, instead of a SECOND full `rfc8785.dumps` of the envelope; halves dumps/frame), tests `test_framing.py` (byte-identity guard: spliced frame == full re-encode across envelope shapes). **Diagnosis:** the batch-drain proving NO gain disproved my earlier asyncio root-cause; profiling pinned the dominant cost as the **pure-Python `rfc8785` JCS encoder** (~75% of writer time). The crc-splice is the safe in-wave win; the residual ceiling is the per-frame canonical encode + §4.2 whitelist walk (both D-7-required), needing a faster-JCS-encoder dependency or a fused encode pass — beyond this wave, surfaced. Behavior-preserving: the count_to_three exact-sequence oracle + all 136 tests + 1 honest perf xfail green; ruff + mypy --strict clean (28 files). Closed clean (perf re-baseline decision pending the Architect).

- **cancel-others / let-finish TerminationPolicy paths (2026-06-13, Wave 9 prerequisite for R-1)** — Wired the kernel §8 / F-LIFE-2 `CANCEL_OTHERS` runtime path the reference topology R-1 needs (it was an enum value with no recipe + no runtime handling). Files: `policies.py` (`cancel_all_others(when)` → CANCEL_OTHERS; `let_finish(when)` → graceful-drain finalise), `runtime.py` (`_consult_termination` CANCEL_OTHERS branch → `_cancel_others`: cancels every live Producer EXCEPT the subject — the producer of the deciding event, read from the lifecycle payload's producer ref per P-SUBJECT-ID since the envelope producer is null for substrate.* events; the cancelled tasks emit `substrate.ProducerCancelled` drained by the still-running writer loop; the run continues to quiescence — non-terminal, idempotent so a repeat match doesn't thrash), `runstate.py` (`task_by_instance` map so the subject can be spared), `api.py` (exports). Tested: candidates cancelled + observable on the log, adjudicator spared, exactly one cancel-others (idempotent), run finalises without hanging on 30s-sleeping candidates. `subtree_cancellation` (the spawn-tree variant) remains a documented F-LIFE-2 deferral (needs the spawn-tree; R-1 doesn't need it). - **Wave 8 — composition (substrate-as-Producer + export maps) (2026-06-13) + handshake Fixes 1/2** — Closes conformance check 7 (export boundary; technical §20, F-COMP-1/2/3). Files: `composition.py` (NEW — `embedded_substrate(topology, *, exports, default_export)` returns a Producer `start` callable that runs an INNER Runtime at its own record root and translates export-mapped inner kinds onto the outer bus; `EmbeddedRunFailed`), `runtime.py` (`_producer_task` threads `inner_run_id` onto `substrate.ProducerFailed`; manifest now records `topology.exports`), `topology.py` (`b.export` docstring — declarative→manifest), `api.py` (exports `embedded_substrate`/`EmbeddedRunFailed` + the public exception hierarchy); tests `test_composition.py`. The translator subscribes (via the read-only follower) to ONLY export-mapped inner kinds and yields outer-schema Structs through ordinary OUTER admission — the entire backpressure story, no second path; the inner run proceeds untouched at its own root. Inner `substrate.*` never cross unless mapped (verified); a mis-declared outer kind becomes `ProducerEmittedInvalidEvent` at the outer boundary (not a crash). Inner-run failure → ONE outer `substrate.ProducerFailed` carrying the inner run_id (taken from the inner RunResult, robust). Nested-depth smoke test (2 levels, 3 roots). **Between-wave independent review (vs §20 + F-COMP + check 7)** found 4 spec divergences, all addressed: (#1 BLOCKER) per-frame inner provenance `{inner_run_id,inner_seq}` can't ride the locked wire ProducerRef → **surfaced as `P-COMPOSITION-INNER-PROVENANCE`** (run-granularity provenance verified: inner root in the outer TriggerFired.resolved_input + inner_run_id on ProducerFailed); (#2 default export = inner RunFinalised) → implemented via `default_export` (author-named outer carrier, since outer kinds can't be substrate.*); (#3 b.export dead) → wired into the RunStarted manifest (`topology.exports`, observable per check 7); (#4 inner_run_id scraped from polled frames) → read from `RunResult.run_id`. **Folded external-handshake #3 Fixes:** Fix 1 — the attach follower now reads INCREMENTALLY (`os.lseek` to the cursor + read only the new tail; O(new) not O(filesize) per poll) and uses a single `framing.recover` `\n`-scan for both hot and sealed segments; Fix 2 — the public exception hierarchy (SubstrateError + 7 subclasses) is re-exported from `api`, and `cli.run` now catches BY TYPE (`except api.BusLockedError`) instead of string-matching class names. Low-priority handshake items (D-8 error-class regex localization-lossiness; `safe_raw` object-address in raw_payload) logged to ## Drift watchlist. Dual contract: signal — composition boundary sequences over real records; artifact — **125 tests, all four gates green, mypy --strict clean (26 files)**. - **runtime.py God-module refactor (2026-06-13)** — Behavior-preserving split of the 752-LOC Runtime God-class (Architect-mandated hard gate before resuming features). New files: `runstate.py` (87 LOC — `RunPhase` enum {RUNNING/PAUSED/FINALISED/FAILED} replacing the four ad-hoc booleans, + `RunState` dataclass holding the ~two-dozen per-run mutable fields, ending two-phase construction on the Runtime instance) and `sequencer.py` (397 LOC — `AppendCycle`, the single-writer six-step append cycle + helpers `_resolve`/`_emit`/`_maybe_offload`/`_resolved_input_fields`/`_track_lifecycle`/`_view_failure`/`_stage_routes`/`_eval_triggers`/`_quarantine` + the `_Emission`/`_Lifecycle` dataclasses + `subscribed()`, operating on a shared RunState). `runtime.py` shrinks 752→410 LOC and keeps only run lifecycle, locking, the writer loop, Producer tasks, and termination — constructing one RunState + one AppendCycle per run() and delegating each event to `self._cyc.cycle()`. `record.py` gains a `put_blob()` Law-of-Demeter passthrough (the runtime no longer reaches `self._record.blobs.put`). The single `_emit` helper builds the envelope dict AND the typed Event from one seq/t/payload (killing the parallel hand-built construction + drift risk). Dual contract: signal — every prior runtime test sequence reproduced unchanged; artifact — **88 tests, all four gates green, mypy --strict clean (22 files)**. Independent review (grounded vs kernel v15 six-step order + tech §6.2/§6.3/§5.2): all 8 behavior-preservation points PRESERVED; the one deliberate change (`_consult_termination` sets FINALISED only `if phase is RUNNING`) is correct + necessary — it compensates for folding the old independent `_terminated`/`_failed` booleans into one enum and HARDENS the view-failure-mid-finalise case (a mid-cascade FAILED is no longer clobberable to finalised). No regression. Wave 6 (replay+inspect) remains held uncommitted until its own commit. Closed clean.

- **Safety net + review-pass-2 correctness (2026-06-13)** — A second external review (run after the Priority-1 commit) surfaced a real non-termination hazard and five correctness defects; fixed in priority order. Files: `pyproject.toml` (pytest-timeout dep + `timeout=30`), `runtime.py`, `policies.py`, `triggers.py`, `topology.py`; new `tests/test_liveness.py`; `tests/test_robustness.py` additions; proposal `P-TRIGGERFIRED-INPUT-BLOB`. **Priority A (safety net):** added `pytest-timeout` + a suite-wide 30s timeout so any never-quiescent writer or self-feeding-Trigger loop FAILS FAST instead of wedging the machine (an earlier non-terminating revision had spun orphaned pytest processes to load-avg 66); added `test_liveness.py` — self-feeding Triggers (a Producer emitting an event its own Trigger subscribes to, the kernel's named divergence hazard) bounded by Once / cooldown / PerKey-dedup, each asserting termination with a finite event count under an explicit per-test timeout. **Priority B (correctness):** (3) **PerKey + cooldown silent data loss** — `policy.admit()` ran BEFORE the cooldown gate, so a cooldown-suppressed cycle still consumed PerKey's seen-key, permanently losing that key's only firing; reordered the cooldown check BEFORE admit (regression test with the consume-then-reappear sequence). (4) **lock-acquire-outside-try fd leak** — moved the flock acquire so a BusLockedError/UnsupportedPlatformError still propagates to the caller (config-time refusal) while a RecordWriter-construction failure after the lock now hits the finally that releases it (no self-deadlock on same-process retry). (5) **swallowed kernel errors** — the bare `except Exception` now binds the exception, appends `substrate.RunFinalised{reason:"kernel_error", error}` to the log (best-effort, never after close/fsync-fail), and yields status=failed — a kernel bug is no longer a silent truncated record. (6) **dead finalisation_payload** — implemented the real mechanism: `TerminationPolicy(finalisation=…)` produces the run's final output at finalise time, sanitized/offloaded, recorded in RunFinalised.finalisation_payload AND surfaced on RunResult; absent → None with no key (not an always-None promise). (7) **overloaded `$blob` sentinel** — the oversized TriggerFired resolved-input field renamed `$blob`→`input_blob` (carrying the BlobRef) to kill the `{"$blob":{"$blob":…}}` field-key collision; the emission-payload blob case keeps the bare BlobRef (no collision there); filed `P-TRIGGERFIRED-INPUT-BLOB`. Dual contract: signal — liveness/cooldown/finalisation/kernel-error sequences asserted on the record; artifact — **87 tests, ruff check + ruff format --check clean, mypy --strict clean (20 files; inspect/replay present in-tree but Wave 6 is committed separately after the runtime refactor gate)**. NEXT (hard gate, Architect-directed): the runtime.py God-module refactor (extract the append-cycle Sequencer, _RunState dataclass, single _emit, RunState enum, RecordWriter.put_blob) BEFORE resuming Wave 6.

- **Wave 6-prep / Priority-1 robustness hardening (2026-06-13)** — Closed the external code review against the live runtime. Files: `runtime.py`, `encoding.py`, `triggers.py`, `topology.py`, `policies.py`, `pyproject.toml`; new tests `test_robustness.py`; proposal `P-TRIGGERFIRED-INSTANCE` filed (+ `P-TRIGGERFIRED-FACTORY` disposition updated). Fixes: (1) **try/finally around the run body** — task cancellation + manifest/`close()` + lock release ALWAYS run; a writer-loop or FsyncError exception no longer leaks tasks or skips finalisation, and yields `RunResult.status="failed"`; dead `else "finalised"` branch removed. (2) **One shared sanitize-or-log helper** (`encoding.try_canonical`/`safe_raw`/`_stringify_non_canonical`) routed through all five user-value ingestion points — emission boundary `raw_payload` (now frame-safe: out-of-range ints/NaN/Inf/non-str-keys/foreign types stringified, since `msgspec.to_builtins` range-checks nothing), resolved trigger input, initial input at bootstrap, and the PerKey firing key — so a non-canonical value becomes a recorded `ProducerEmittedInvalidEvent`/`InputBuildFailed`, never an uncaught writer crash. (3) **Blob offload wired** (`BLOB_THRESHOLD_BYTES`): emissions AND `TriggerFired.resolved_input` over 16 KiB canonical bytes are write-ahead-stored and replaced with a `$blob` reference before framing (tech §3.7/§3.3, D-5 — `$blob` is a top-level field mutually exclusive with `resolved_input`; `input_sha256` still hashes the original canonical bytes). (4) **Four silent no-ops wired:** `persistent=True` acquires/releases the flock (Windows → `UnsupportedPlatformError`); trigger-level `Logical(n)` cooldown enforced in the runtime, counted in **subscription-matching** appends (kernel §6), with `WhileTrue` self-throttle removed so there is exactly one enforcement point (the builder lifts a `WhileTrue` policy cooldown to the trigger level); `quiescence_with_watchdog(seconds=)` drives `_poll_s` (bounded by the fine-grained default so a large window never delays detection); view-failure `RunFinalised{reason:view_failure}` emitted from step 3 with a `_terminated` guard in `_cycle` + `_control.clear()` so nothing follows the terminal (RUN-BOUNDARY at-most-once). (5) **ruff gates reconciled** — `extend-exclude=["kernel_spec","proof"]` + `ruff format` over the package; `ruff check` AND `ruff format --check` now BOTH exit-0. (6) `topology.view()` rejects an empty Subscription like trigger/route. Dual contract: signal — view-failure/cooldown/blob sequences asserted on the record; artifact — **59 tests, ruff check + ruff format --check clean, mypy --strict clean (18 files)**. Two independent adversarial review passes (Rubber Duck): the first found 2 blockers (double-finalise after view failure; `$blob` nested inside `resolved_input`) + majors (raw-seq cooldown count, double WhileTrue cooldown, hash-from-unsealed); all addressed; the second pass verified all five fixes HOLD with no new issues. `factory`/`instance` on `TriggerFired` carried implemented-ahead-of-ratification under the proposal taxonomy (instance is Architect-directed, closes F-OBS-2/check 11). Closed clean.

## Deferred

*Anyone may append. Re-visit conditions noted.*

- **2026-08-03 — RESOLVED (was accepted risk): a delegate child that outruns `timeout_seconds` is now CANCELLED cooperatively, not abandoned (review F-8).** The Architect said fix it, not carry it. `_run_child_to_answer` runs the child on an event loop the caller holds a handle to; on timeout it `call_soon_threadsafe(task.cancel)` across the thread boundary, `Runtime.run`'s finally cancels the child's producers and SEALS its record, and the parent records `ToolResult(ok=False)`. The orphan stops writing; its sealed record (no FinalAnswer) and the parent's timeout agree. Test `test_timed_out_child_is_cancelled_and_its_record_sealed` proves the record does not grow after cancellation. The original accepted-risk note is kept below as the audit trail.
- **2026-08-02 — ACCEPTED RISK (SUPERSEDED by the 2026-08-03 resolution above): a delegate child that outruns `timeout_seconds` is ABANDONED, not cancelled (review F-8, moved out of the "closed" column by the verification pass).** A Python worker thread cannot be killed, so on timeout `delegate` raises `TimeoutError` naming the child root and returns to the parent while the orphan keeps running to its OWN bounded termination — under `run_tool_agent.py --delegate` it holds the parent's suite rooted at the parent's workdir, so it keeps writing to the shared workspace and consuming model calls after the parent has recorded failure. The two records are now LEGIBLE about the disagreement (the error names the root) but still disagree. **Why accepted for now:** the child's natural bound is `child_max_steps × the model-call timeout`, and both defaults + `run_tool_agent` set `timeout_seconds` ABOVE that (`timeout*(max_steps+1)`), so a timeout is the pathological case, not the norm. **The real fix (re-visit condition):** cooperative cancellation INSIDE the child run loop (a deadline the child's producers check), not in the joining thread — do it if delegate moves to a concurrent-child (`embedded_substrate`-Producer) shape, or if an orphan is ever observed corrupting a shared workspace in practice. Honest status: mitigated + disclosed, NOT fixed.

- **2026-06-25 — INTAKE: external "is it right / best-practice / avant-garde / real science" review of the assay layer (record-and-revisit-before-the-first-SCORED-run; not fixing now per Architect "move on").** Verdict: measurement architecture SOUND; genuinely best practice (rare here); **NOT avant-garde — drop that framing.** It is a disciplined IMPORT of established experimental rigor, not new science: paired McNemar = Dietterich 1998; pre-registration + power/MDE-before-running + multiplicity = Card et al. EMNLP 2020 (50+ yrs in med/psych/econ); placebo/ablation = Lipton & Steinhardt 2018, Adebayo et al. NeurIPS 2018. The norm it beats: agent frameworks report on contaminated static benchmarks with no controls (Kapoor & Narayanan "AI Agents That Matter" 2024; simple baselines beat complex agents; multi-agent gains explained by unaccounted compute). Honest label: "a strong, disciplined application of known best practice." Only arguably-ahead bits: refusing to score dynamics topologies on QA accuracy, and the run-and-observe/replayable flag in the data type — and even those aren't unprecedented (construct validity). **SIX real cracks to fix BEFORE running for score:** **(1, sharpest for coding) FIREWALL feedback tests from grading tests** — coding_flow's correction loop feeds gate failures back to the drafter; in `bench_coding.py` the gate tests ARE the grading tests, so the loop is taught to the test (its "win" = leakage). The agent's visible tests MUST be disjoint from the oracle's FAIL_TO_PASS/PASS_TO_PASS. **(2) attribute the win to the MECHANISM, not sample count** — baseline(1 draft) vs full(N drafts+correction) confounds structure with "more attempts"; add the ablation arms the design already names (single-model best-of-N at N samples; N-drafts-no-correction). NB this is CONSTRUCT VALIDITY via ablation, NOT cost/matched-compute (no money, no compute-budget normalization — that stays dead). **(3) the null rule is statistically wrong** — non-significance ≠ equivalence; "report NULL if CI crosses zero" must become a TOST equivalence test against a pre-specified margin; consider FDR over Holm. **(4) pass^k under-propagates trial variance** — collapsing k trials to one boolean then feeding McNemar treats an estimate as an observation (anti-conservative); needs a paired two-level bootstrap on Δ-pass^k or a reliability/random-effects model, and exact/Bayesian binomial intervals over a flat bootstrap (Miller, "Adding Error Bars to Evals," 2024). **(5) "no model in the scorer" = determinism, not validity** — SWE-Bench+ (Aleithan et al.) found leak/weak tests inflating resolved ~3×; a deterministic grader can be deterministically wrong → drop "ground truth" language, add a grader-validity axis + report residual oracle error. **(6) a delta is model-dependent, not a property of the topology** — one result = "beats THIS control on THIS split with THIS model" (Tran & Kiela); decontaminated ≠ externally valid. **Minor:** name the best-of-N selector (oracle pass@k upper-bound vs a real selector measure different things); git pre-registration is only a tamper-evident timestamp; "post-cutoff" is perishable (needs a refresh cadence). **Citation blemishes the audit caught (none move a verdict):** arXiv 2409.07638 misattributed to a phantom "Miao et al."; a MetaGPT figure conflating HumanEval 85.9 with MBPP 87.7; a "Validity" preprint over-stretched. Re-visit each crack when wiring the first SCORED benchmark; (1) and (2) determine whether it measures structure or measures leakage+sampling.

## Open questions

*Anyone may append.*

- **B-Q-1.** Package/import name — **DEFERRED** 2026-06-12 (see `## Deferred`). Working name "Substrate" stands; official name picked later. Was: product D-2 shortlist (`substrate-kernel`, `substrate-bus`, `pysubstrate`, `substrated`, `horizon-substrate`, `buskernel`); design-spec placeholder `rostrum`; brainstorm added Strata / Keel / Chronicle / Cradle. **Constraint added 2026-07-22 (audit):** "Substrate" collides hard with Parity's Substrate, the widely-known Rust blockchain framework — treat the collision as disqualifying for any public/package release; the rename decision inherits this as a hard input.

- **SWE-Q-1 (2026-06-27, solver) — the SELECT reproduction signal FALSE-POSITIVES; keep it, demote it, or strengthen it?** The model-generated reproduction test only exercises the part of the issue the model understood/fixed, so it reports "Issue resolved" for a partial fix (proven on the 480B flask-4045 run — SELECT was green on regression+repro, oracle said unresolved). It's a useful TIEBREAK (it did discriminate slot 0 from slot 1) but NOT a "resolved" certifier. Options: keep as a low-weight tiebreak (current), demote below regression entirely, or strengthen the repro prompt to enumerate multiple failure modes from the issue. The oracle is the only truth; internal signals SELECT, they don't CERTIFY. Reviewer input wanted before changing SELECT's signal weighting.

- **SWE-Q-2 (2026-06-27, solver) — `exclude` uses test_patch FILE PATHS (grade metadata); keep-with-disclosure or drop?** The proximity picker drops the held-out test_patch file paths from the regression set — path-only metadata, never reaching the solver, disclosed per-instance via `exclude_delta` (how often it bites beyond proximity). It modestly spares a correct patch from an issue-related test that proximity MISSES (non-co-located). Reviewer #69 leaned keep-and-measure; SWE-Q is whether a clean SELF-CONTAINED number should drop it (proximity-alone) for the eventual reported rate. Not blocking the exploratory work.

## Drift watchlist

*Agent maintains. Patterns to monitor across sprints. When the same observation surfaces in three consecutive sprints, escalate to `## Surfaced for review`.*

- **2026-06-27 (Agent, full SWE-bench Lite run) — any reported resolve-rate on a PUBLIC static set is partly memorization; the contamination guard is FRESHNESS, not just the firewall.** SWE-bench Lite is public and in every training set, so some of the 108/300 resolves are recall, not capability — the per-instance firewall (hide the graded tests from the solver) does NOT fix this; it's the global version of the same problem. The decided escape is **public + post-cutoff/dated + self-run** (e.g. SWE-bench-Live), NOT a private/held-out set (which trades contamination for an unverifiable vendor number — the SWE-bench Pro critique). Watch: before any resolve-rate is reported as a capability claim (not just a plumbing check), it must run on a dated set whose problems postdate the model's training cutoff; until then, label every public-Lite number "contamination-exposed." Ties to memory `project-benchmarking-power-reality` + the firewall.

- **2026-06-27 (Agent, full-run instrumentation) — distinguish GRADE-infrastructure failure from topology failure, always.** The full run's only 5 non-results were missing SWE-bench eval images (`BuildImageError/404`), which the runner first lumped as `error` alongside genuine clone/topology failures — a half-hour of looking like "our topology can't run on sympy" when 71/74 sympy graded fine. Fixed: `grade_unavailable` is now its own status (the topology produced a patch; only grading failed). Watch: any new benchmark backend must keep the "topology ran but couldn't be graded" bucket separate from "topology/clone broke" — conflating them mis-attributes upstream infra gaps to our code (the exact thing the instrumentation exists to prevent).

- **2026-06-13 (Agent, post-1.0 demo session) — N-PERF-1 is hardware-variant; the floor is not portable, which is exactly why it is out of CI.** The same append-rate probe measures **~56K appends/sec on the build machine** (passes the A2-recalibrated 40K floor) and **~26K on this laptop** (fails it). This is not a regression — it is the per-frame pure-Python rfc8785 (JCS) canonical encode (D-7-required for byte-identity) being CPU-bound and machine-dependent. Design consequence already in place: conformance check 15 runs `--no-perf` in CI (so a slow runner never red-flags the suite) and the floor gate is opt-in locally (`SUBSTRATE_PERF_GATE`) / run on controlled hardware at release; `docs/demo.md` states the laptop number and the failure plainly rather than hiding it. Watch: if anyone proposes putting the perf floor back into the CI matrix, this is why not. The post-1.0 lever if a deterministic firehose ever needs more is a compiled (C/Rust) JCS encoder — do NOT hand-write one (byte-identity contract).

- **2026-06-13 (handshake low-priority a) — D-8 error-class regex is localization-lossy for non-standard exception names.** `inspect._EXC_CLASS_RE` matches names ending Error/Exception/Warning/Interrupt; a custom exception class (`class Boom(Exception)`) or `SystemExit`/`StopIteration` falls back to the `<error>` sentinel. NOT a correctness bug (two such errors still compare equal under D-8; the relation stays sound) — only LOSSY localization (a Boom-vs-KeyError divergence reads as `<error>` vs `<error>` and won't localize). Revisit if a topology surfaces custom exception classes whose divergence must localize; broaden the regex or carry the class name explicitly then.

- **2026-06-13 (handshake low-priority b) — `safe_raw` raw_payload can embed an object address for a non-canonical OBJECT emission.** On `substrate.ProducerEmittedInvalidEvent`, a non-canonical emission of a foreign OBJECT (not a dict/list/scalar) is preserved verbatim via `safe_raw` → `repr(obj)`, which for a plain object embeds its memory address (`<X object at 0x...>`). That `raw_payload` is on a `substrate.*` frame and is NOT D-8-normalized, so two runs emitting the same bad object could false-diverge on the address. Extremely narrow (requires emitting a non-Struct, non-whitelist object and then D-8-comparing two such runs); lowest priority. Fix-if-it-bites: route `raw_payload` reprs through the same class-preserving/address-stripping normalization, or D-8-normalize `raw_payload`.

- **2026-06-13 (Agent) — composition per-frame inner provenance `{inner_run_id, inner_seq}` not stamped (§20).** The boundary translator yields bare outer Structs; the locked wire ProducerRef `{kind,instance,parent}` has no metadata slot and outer kinds can't be `substrate.*`, so per-FRAME inner provenance has nowhere to ride. Run-granularity provenance HOLDS (inner root in the outer TriggerFired.resolved_input; inner_run_id on ProducerFailed). Surfaced as `P-COMPOSITION-INNER-PROVENANCE` (proposals.json) for an Architect ruling + likely vocab bump. Watch for a reader needing to trace an outer event to an exact inner seq.

- **2026-06-13 (Agent) — ~~`b.export` vs `embedded_substrate(exports=)` two-source-of-truth~~ → RESOLVED 2026-06-13.** Was: two parallel export-map sources could drift. RESOLVED in the export single-source reconciliation (see ## Built): `b.export` + `Registration.exports` REMOVED; the export map is declared once on `embedded_substrate(exports=)` and the manifest DERIVES from it (per-kind `producer_kinds[].export_map` + the topology aggregate). One source; drift impossible.

- **`view_at` instance-vs-name signature (tech §16 flow-back).** §16 specifies `view_at(record, seq, view: str)`; implemented as `view_at(record, seq, view: View-instance)` because a record stores no View code. Documented flow-back (amendment A1.c); no code change.

- **Vocabulary-already-in-spec.** The kernel spec pre-names lifecycle kinds and the envelope. Watch that Sprint-0 layers 0–2 transcribe the spec faithfully rather than re-inventing or silently renaming (e.g., spec says `ProducerEmittedInvalidEvent`, not `InvalidEmission`).
- **Orchestration vs. kit purity.** We are layering teams + worktrees + best-of-N on top of a kit that defers these to Section 3. Watch that the orchestration never bypasses the dual contract, the Rubber Duck Pass, or the vocabulary lock (hard rule 10 — no silent hand-author).
- **Producer protocol shape (design §9.6 callable ⇆ tech §16 `.start` method).** Implementation follows design §9.6 (a Producer is a callable `(input)->AsyncIterable[Event]`, factory returns it) — reasoned and asyncio-native. Flow-back: technical §16 should show the callable form, not the object-with-`.start()` form.
- **Input-sealing accepted set (tech §8.3).** Implementation seals dict/list into immutable forms (MappingProxyType / tuple) so design §3.3's ergonomic dict inputs work while staying immutable-by-construction; §8.3 lists frozen-only. Flow-back: §8.3 should list immutable-sealed dict/list among accepted forms.
- **Seal-on-roll vs seal-on-finalise (design §3.2 ⇆ tech §2).** Implementation seals segments on size-roll only; a complete record keeps its hot segment `.open` and is recognised by a terminal `substrate.RunFinalised` frame. Matches the design-spec example; tech §2 prose says "seal on finalise." Candidate clarification for a tech-spec revision (flow-back note); watch that no reader assumes a complete record has no `.open` segment.

- **TriggerFired carries `factory`+`instance` (strict validator-extras).** The runtime emits both on every TriggerFired but the locked v0.1 TriggerFired schema declares neither; lifecycle events bypass `_resolve`'s schema validation so nothing catches this at runtime. Filed `P-TRIGGERFIRED-FACTORY` (pre-existing) + `P-TRIGGERFIRED-INSTANCE` (Architect-directed, closes F-OBS-2/check 11). Carried implemented-ahead-of-ratification per the P-SUBJECT-ID precedent. Escalate at the v0.2 version bump / a conformance harness that validates lifecycle frames against the manifest schemas — until then these frames would fail a strict re-validation against the v0.1 schema.

- **Lifecycle payloads bypass the §4.2 whitelist.** `_resolve`'s `_Lifecycle` branch returns runtime-built payloads unchecked. Realistic exposure is small (`measured_us` in range, error strings fine), but a runtime-built payload with an out-of-range value would crash at frame time inside `_cycle` and land in the writer-fail path rather than being recorded. Watch when new lifecycle fields are added; consider routing lifecycle payloads through `try_canonical` too if any numeric/foreign field appears.

- **D-8 comparison normalizes away run-varying identity (run_id, instance ULIDs).** `inspect.first_divergence` / `_payload_hash` strip `run_id` and `instance` and reduce `producer` refs to `{kind}` before hashing, so two runs of the *same deterministic topology* are D-8-equivalent (else they'd always diverge at frame 0 on run_id, defeating conformance check 13 "localize the perturbed *decision*"). The spec's D-8 is "(event-kind sequence, decision-identity sequence, canonical payload hashes), supplementary metadata excluded" — it names t/host/config but does NOT enumerate run_id/instance. The exact exclusion set is an implementation refinement; flag as a tech-spec §14 flow-back (pin the D-8 normalization set: run_id, per-Producer instance/parent ULIDs, t). Watch that `input_sha256` and `firing_key` (the actual decision identities) are NOT stripped.

- **view_at takes a View instance, not a view name (tech §16 signature drift).** The spec §16 signature is `view_at(record, seq, view: str)` (by name, assuming the topology's View code is available). Implemented as `view_at(record, seq, view: View)` (an instance) because a record stores event payloads, not View code — the caller must supply the View whose update()/subscription define the fold. Tech-spec §14/§16 flow-back: show the instance form (or document that the name form requires a live topology registry). Already noted in inspect.py.

- **PredicateQuarantined emits union fields, not the discriminated shape.** `_quarantine` always sets `measured_us`+`k` even on the `reason:"exception"` path (locked schema marks them budget-only; `error` exception-only). Known soft spot — the vocab flags it as a PAYLOAD_FIELD_PROPOSED discriminated schema. Reconcile when that proposal is ratified.

## Sprint tail

*Agent maintains. Last 10 sprint summaries; older entries roll into `## Built` as compressed paragraphs.*

### Sprint 076 (2026-09-14, closed) — `SessionRegistry.interrupt(tier)` + `InterruptRequested` envelope kind
- **Scope:** pair the daemon-side interrupt path with the two-tier ESC surface the substrate-ui shell now ships (its Phase 8 items 5 and 6). `SessionRegistry.interrupt(session_id, *, tier: str = "hard")` grows a `tier` argument. `tier="hard"` (the pre-Phase-8 default; every existing caller passes no kwarg and gets the same semantics) walks `kind_by_instance` for `"model"` then `"tool"` and cancels the first live producer through `Runtime.cancel_producer` with `caller=f"daemon:interrupt-{tier}"`. `tier="soft"` cancels a live model (soft on a model is a graceful stop — same primitive), and for a live TOOL it does NOT cancel; it returns a synthetic ref `{"kind": "signal", "instance": "soft", "parent": None}` so the caller renders `^C — soft interrupt requested; the model will stop after this tier` rather than the pre-Phase-8 `^C — no turn in flight`. `substrate/topologies/session` grows `InterruptRequested(session_id: str, tier: str, source: str)` as a frozen msgspec.Struct + `__all__` export.
- **The tool_loop unblock (companion commit 806c6680).** The user's own ESC-during-`bash for i in {1..10}; sleep 1; done` paste surfaced that the tool producer at `tool_loop/__init__.py:306` called `entry.run(args)` synchronously on the runtime's event loop. `subprocess.run` pinned the loop for the full ten seconds; `SessionRegistry.interrupt`'s `call_soon_threadsafe(_do_cancel)` closure never got a slice; its 1s future timed out; the daemon returned `None`; the ui-server responded `{interrupted: false}`; the client rendered `no turn in flight`. Wrapped in `await asyncio.to_thread(entry.run, args)`. The tool body still runs on a worker thread synchronously; the loop keeps its slice; cancel / soft-signal closures land within their 1s window. Tool contract unchanged.
- **Dual contract:**
  - *Signal.* Pre-Phase-8: `POST /api/session/<id>/interrupt` → `{"interrupted": bool, "landed": bool, ...}`. Phase-8: the same endpoint reads `?tier=soft|hard` (default `hard`), the response gains `{"tier": <str>, "signal": <bool>}`. `interrupted=true` covers both a real producer cancel and a soft-signal accept. Client is source-compatible.
  - *Artifact.* `SessionRegistry.interrupt` signature widens with a keyword-only `tier`. All existing call sites pass no tier and get `hard` (identical to pre-Phase-8 behaviour). `InterruptRequested` Struct constructs (`from substrate.topologies.session import InterruptRequested; InterruptRequested(session_id='s_xxx', tier='soft', source='daemon:interrupt-soft')` verified in-session).
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Narration.* A session runs a bash tool. The user hits ESC. Client POSTs `/api/session/<id>/interrupt?tier=hard`. Server calls `_SESSION_REGISTRY.interrupt(session_id, tier="hard")`. The daemon schedules `_do_cancel` via `call_soon_threadsafe`; the loop, freed by the `to_thread`-wrapped tool body, runs the closure; the closure finds `kind_by_instance = {"i0": "tool"}`; the model arm skips (no live model); the tool arm calls `runtime.cancel_producer("i0", cause="external", caller="daemon:interrupt-hard")`; the task cancels; `_producer_task`'s CancelledError handler writes `substrate.ProducerCancelled` on the record; the session topology's `park-on-interrupt` trigger fires the park producer; a `Park(reason="interrupted")` envelope lands. Server returns `{interrupted: true, landed: true, tier: "hard", signal: false, producer: <ref>}`. Client renders `^C — hard interrupt; producer cancelled`. The tape order is `TURN_INTERRUPTED → SESSION_END_REQUESTED → STREAM_CLOSED` — verified end-to-end.
  - *Observations (six).* missing pair — none (every ProducerStarted has its ProducerCompleted or ProducerCancelled). order violation — none (the CancelledError writes ProducerCancelled before the finally-block clears the instance from kind_by_instance). vocabulary gap — `InterruptRequested` is declared as a session-topology Struct + `__all__` export but is NOT yet registered under a `producer_kind` and has no producer that emits it and no trigger that subscribes to it. The docstring names the gap inline: injection path (daemon writes it to the record when tier=soft with a live tool) and the fragment producer that subscribes to it are follow-up items — filed as `## Surfaced for review` under the phase-8 substrate-ui checkpoint. payload anomaly — none (soft-tier signal ref matches the ProducerRef shape at type level). timing surprise — the loop unblock is the whole reason a 1s future does not time out any more. tone trace — the docstring names "soft" and "hard" as the two tiers; the caller string is `daemon:interrupt-<tier>` so the ProducerCancelled envelope carries which tier fired. Grep clean for "gesticulating" / "cogitating" / other decorative verbs.
  - *Dispositions.* Clean. The soft-with-live-tool signal path is documented as returning a synthetic ref rather than a real ProducerRef; caller reads `ref["kind"] == "signal"` to distinguish. `noted`, by design. Zero `halted`.
- **Files:** `src/substrate/session_registry.py`, `src/substrate/topologies/session/__init__.py`, `src/substrate/topologies/tool_loop/__init__.py` (the `to_thread` wrap).
